Above 28, `GammaFunction::logValue`'s old fit made every term about 1e-10 too small, which hid it; with #150's fix alone it would have been 32,025 in all. It now returns 1 where the sum from the right falls short only through rounding, and takes the term at zero as `exp(-lambda)` where the steps reach it. |
| [#150](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/150) (2026-11-11) | `ql/math/distributions/gammadistribution.{hpp,cpp}` | `GammaFunction::logValue` was Numerical Recipes' six-term Lanczos fit, off by up to 2.0e-10 against mpmath (1.0e-10 by 100, 8.8e-13 at 11), and `value()`, `Factorial::get` above 27, the incomplete gamma and beta functions and the distributions, quadrature rules, Bessel functions, engines and meshers built on them carried that as a relative error. It now returns `lgamma_r`, the reentrant lgamma, within 1.7 ulps, at 130 instructions per call against 160; `std::lgamma` writes the global `signgam`, which halted TSan in 5 of 5 runs with two threads. `value()` stays exp(logValue) from 1, now within 1.2e-13 relative below 171.6. |
| [#186](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/186) (2026-11-11) | `ql/math/integrals/gaussianquadratures.cpp` | `TabulatedGaussLegendre`'s tables were Abramowitz and Stegun's to 15 decimals, off by up to 7.4e-16 with errors that don't cancel (order 20's weights summed to 2 - 2.0e-15), so its rules missed polynomials they integrate exactly by up to 33 eps of the sum, against 2.4 for tables rounded to the nearest double. They are now the nodes and weights at 60 digits, given to 20 significant digits, which round to the nearest double. `BivariateCumulativeNormalDistributionWe04DP`, which uses orders 6, 12 and 20, moves by at most 1.9e-16, and at order 20 toward the closed form at a = b = 0 (2.4e-15 to 3.3e-16 relative at rho = -0.9). |
| [#193](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/193) (2026-11-11) | `ql/pricingengines/blackcalculator.{hpp,cpp}` | `BlackCalculator::vanna` and `volga` used the vanilla option's formulas for every payoff, so they were wrong, sign included, for cash-or-nothing, asset-or-nothing and gap payoffs, and `volga` was vega's derivative in the standard deviation, off by √T. Both now come from each payoff's alpha and beta, with the payoff's jump at the strike carried in a new protected member, `jump_`, so that a vanilla option's terms don't have to cancel; `volga` is taken in the volatility. Vanilla vanna and volga (the parent's times √T) move only by the old path's rounding, toward 50-digit values (9.3e-11 to 6.9e-14 at worst). At a zero strike they stay NaN with six other Greeks (#201). |
| [#201](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/201) (2026-11-11) | `ql/pricingengines/blackcalculator.cpp` | At a strike `close()` takes for zero, `BlackCalculator::initialize` takes d1 and d2 as infinite and the densities as zero, and `gamma`, `gammaForward`, `theta`, `vega`, `strikeSensitivity`, `strikeGamma`, `vanna` and `volga` multiplied those zeros by something infinite: NaN for every payoff, and through `AnalyticEuropeanEngine` for a call struck at zero. The Greeks now share `initialize()`'s test for the zero strike and take their zero-volatility branches there, which give the limits (zero, or -D for a vanilla call's strike sensitivity, and theta from the value and delta). Nothing else moves. |
| [#200](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/200) (2026-11-11) | `ql/math/incompletegamma.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/math/distributions/gammadistribution.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/math/distributions/poissondistribution.hpp` | `incompleteGammaFunction`, `CumulativeGammaDistribution` (so `CumulativeChiSquareDistribution`) and `CumulativePoissonDistribution` were Numerical Recipes' series and continued fraction, stopped after 100 terms. Near the mean they ran out: they threw from a shape of 156, a Poisson mean of 159 and 2,000 degrees of freedom. The gamma CDF's series also stopped at 3e-7 of its sum, so it was off by up to 1.3e-6, and the Poisson CDF, as 1 - P, came out 0 in its left tail where it is 1.9e-22. All three now come from Boost's `gamma_p` and `gamma_q` under the default policy, as the library's other callers use them: within 1.1e-16 up to a shape of 1,000 and 2e-14 at 1e6. The Poisson CDF is Q itself, through a new `incompleteGammaFunctionComplement`. `incompleteGammaFunction` keeps its `accuracy` and `maxIteration` arguments and ignores them, and the `...SeriesRepr` and `...ContinuedFractionRepr` functions are unchanged. A call costs 3 to 18 times as much (150 ns to 1.35 us), and Boost 1.83 throws its own `evaluation_error` near the mean from a shape of about 2e10. |
| [#151](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/151) (2026-11-11) | `ql/math/modifiedbessel.{hpp,cpp}` | `modifiedBesselFunction_i` switched at \|z\| = 13 from its power series to 30 terms of the large-argument expansion whatever the order, and the complex `modifiedBesselFunction_k` took the same 30 terms past 13. At large orders both were off by any amount (the weighted real I was -7.2e23 at order 50, x = 13, where 7.5e-30 is right), the complex I was off by O(1) for arg z from -pi to -pi/2 past 13, except at half-integer orders, where it took DLMF 10.40.5's form for arg z > -pi/2, the unweighted real I was infinite from x = 710, and the complex K lost up to 3.9e-4 below 13. I now comes from its series while \|z\|^2 <= 4 (\|nu\| + 1), then from the expansions where their sums converge cleanly, then from Boost's `cyl_bessel_i` on the real axis, or, on a complex argument, from Thompson and Barnett's algorithm, scaled by exp(z): Temme's series or Steed's continued fraction for K, K's recurrence, and I from its continued fraction and the Wronskian. The complex K comes from the expansion or the same algorithm, and the left half-plane from -z on the side of the cut that Im z's sign gives. Measured against mpmath on 9,843 points with orders -50.5 to 300 and \|z\| to 1,000: within 22 QL_EPSILON times the condition number, and 6.1 times it away from that algorithm. A complex I through it costs 1.3 to 2.7 us at orders up to 50 and 9.7 us at 300, against 0.27 to 0.42 for the old, wrong values; at 20 of the Heston suite's own arguments the complex I takes 119 ns against 111. |
| [#154](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/154) (2026-11-11) | `ql/math/optimization/simplex.cpp` | With every vertex at the same cost, `Simplex::minimize` counted a reflection costing the same as no worse than the best vertex, and tried the expansion instead of the contraction. When that cost no less, nothing changed, and every later iteration repeated the same steps until the iteration limit: NR's amoeba stops once the costs agree to within its tolerance, but QuantLib's 2007 criterion on the simplex's size has no such exit. GARCH's calibration, at a rootEpsilon of 1e-8, ran all 10,000 iterations in 134 of 5,259 minimizations, and since #121's row `2844` a calibration from a guess was refused there (35, 11 and 3 of 1,125 at 1e-8, 1e-7 and 1e-6). When neither point replaces the worst vertex the simplex now contracts, and shrinks if that fails. Every other state is unchanged, so minimizations that didn't end at the limit are the same to the bit; the 134 end at StationaryPoint after 108 to 511 iterations, at the same cost or one lower by at most 7.9e-16 relative, and no calibration from a guess is refused. In the test suite, built as the release preset builds it, only `testCallableSwapAnderson`'s exercise fits move, whose piecewise-constant cost stalled all 27 of each case until their limit of 1,000; they stop after 20 to 29 iterations, and the six cases take 3.0-3.7 s, where they took 5.2-6.1 s. Which minimizations stall depends on the build's rounding: in CI's coverage and tsan builds one of `testFlatExtrapolation`'s fits does too, and now stops at StationaryPoint, with the coverage flags at the same cost and parameters, bit for bit, so `ceac6144c` lets upstream's check of how each fit ends accept either. |
| [#159](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/159) (2026-11-11) | `ql/experimental/credit/{defaultlossmodel,binomiallossmodel,recursivelossmodel,saddlepointlossmodel}.hpp` | A tranche's expected shortfall is now the mean of its percentile over the levels above, on three loss models that got it wrong. `SaddlePointLossModel` compared the percentile with the tranche's width applied twice and returned it (80 above a percentile of 256 on a tranche of 400), and otherwise integrated the untranched portfolio's tail (117 on a tranche of 100, 585,560 at 99.99 %). It now takes the percentile plus the expected excess over it, divided by 1 - p, from the model's own expansions. The excess is floored at 0 at each factor, where those expansions disagree (#204), and the result is capped at the tranche. `BinomialLossModel` and `RecursiveLossModel` read before the distribution's first point at levels below the probability of no loss (3,307 on a tranche of 400), and drew chords across the kinks at the attachment and detachment (98.58 below a percentile of 100). Both now share one percentile and its exact mean (`detail::trancheLossPercentile`, `detail::trancheLossExpectedShortfall`). The recursive percentile no longer returns a probability at 0 and 1, nor the binomial one the untranched loss at 1. The saddle point's percentile after a default is a fraction of the capped tranche, where it came out 2.1-2.9 % high, and its shortfall on the evaluation date is 0 where it threw. On senior tranches the saddle point's shortfall is still low (#204); `Distribution::expectedShortfall`'s are #203. Since the row dated 2026-11-16 for #281 the excess at each factor comes from the same approximation as the probability, and is kept to [0, max(D - level, 0)]; since the row dated 2026-11-17 for #205 both are exact up to the two smallest losses together, and above that the excess is held between lines through the exact regions' excesses. [#203](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/203)'s row records its fix. |
| [#160](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/160) (2026-11-11) | `ql/experimental/credit/saddlepointlossmodel.hpp` | `SaddlePointLossModel` bracketed its search for the saddle point with bounds worked out from the name with the largest loss alone, which aren't bounds where names differ: on 19 names that lose 80 to 99 each, `Basket::probOverLoss` reached 2.13e7, and was outside [0, 1] at 327 of 401 levels of 0–3 %. The bracket now widens from s = 0 until K′ crosses the level, and where the level is beyond every loss the factor makes possible, or below those it makes certain, the limits are taken. Up to the smallest loss of a name, and above the total less it, the loss distribution given the factor is taken exactly. There the expansions had put the probability of any loss at 0.74 where it is 0.44, so the percentile threw "root not bracketed" below about 0.26 on equity tranches of #159's pool, and the shortfall at 30 % was 27 % low on 0–3 %; it is now within 0.1 %. On the names that differ, the shortfall was within 4.3 % of the exact one on three equity tranches under the Gaussian copula, where it had been up to 54 % high; after the rows dated 2026-11-16 for #306, #281 and #282 it was within 1.5 %, and since the row dated 2026-11-17 for #205 it is within 0.61 %, and exact on 0–3 %. It still fell a little where the level crosses the smallest loss, by up to 3 % of the tranche, until the row dated 2026-11-16 for #281, since which it rose there, by more than the exact one, until the row dated 2026-11-17 for [#205](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/205), since which it doesn't step there. Two tranches of #159's pool lost a cancellation of errors: at 30 % and 50 %, 3–6 % was 6.4 % high where it had been 1.2 %, and 40–60 % 14.7 % low where it had been 11.4 % ([#204](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/204)). Those were five years out; one year out, just before the row dated 2026-11-16 for #281, they were 63 % high and 29 % low. That row took them to 4.4 % and 2.6 % low five years out and 7.1 % and 3.7 % low one year out, and since the row dated 2026-11-17 for #205 3–6 % matches the exact shortfall to within 3e-16 relative, and 40–60 % is the same to six digits. Found since: within rounding of the loss of the names a factor makes certain to default its search still runs, where the tail probability is NaN and the percentile throws ([#280](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/280)); at the top of the region it searches the cumulants overflow or lose every digit ([#282](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/282)); one name that loses nothing takes its exact regions away from the whole pool ([#283](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/283)); and below a factor's expected loss, the cut-off it kept returns 0 where the probability is near 1 ([#281](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/281)). [#280](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/280)'s, [#283](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/283)'s, [#281](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/281)'s and [#282](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/282)'s rows record their fixes. |
| [#161](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/161) (2026-11-11) | `ql/experimental/credit/{basket.hpp,basket.cpp,defaultlossmodel.hpp,defaultprobabilitylatentmodel.hpp,integralntdengine.cpp,nthtodefault.hpp}` | An nth to default on a basket with a default. `Basket::probAtLeastNEvents` handed the contract's rank to the loss model as it was, where `probsBeingNthEvent` takes off the defaults so far, and `DefaultLatentModel` counted a defaulted name as one that could still default. So on eight names with one defaulted, a second to default priced at 0.0353, where as a first to default on the seven left it's 0.0836. The basket now gives 1 up to the defaults so far and asks the model for the rest, and the latent model counts only the names left: the eight give the seven's values to every digit printed. A rank the defaults have reached has no premium or protection left and no fair premium, so `NthToDefault::fairPremium()` refuses, as `SyntheticCDO`'s does on a zero premium leg, where it priced as if live (0.090778 at rank 1); a zero premium rate refuses too, where it gave NaN. The per-name recovery branch ([#208](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/208)), the random models ([#207](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/207)) and the upfront ([#206](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/206)) were left, and fixed later; on `RandomLossLM` the engine still throws, with a message saying why since [#234](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/234), and the full fix is [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250). Upstream's master has the same code. [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250)'s row records its fix. |
| [#162](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/162) (2026-11-11) | `ql/experimental/credit/randomdefaultlatentmodel.hpp` | The random models' probability of each name being the nth to default. `probsBeingNthEvent` put a path's defaults in a map keyed by the day, so a second name defaulting on a day was dropped and every later default moved up a place; at correlation 0.9 on twenty names, the probabilities of being the 20th added up to 0.0057, where P(at least 20) is 0.0102. The models simulate defaults to the day, so where the nth falls among same-day defaults each now takes an equal share, and every path with n defaults or more adds exactly 1 across the names: the sums equal P(at least n) on the same paths. Names moved even where nothing was dropped, as the map credited the lowest-numbered name of a tie (name 0's probability of being the 20th doubled). The loss split's order on a tied day ([#209](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/209)) was left, and fixed later. The vector's layout, left for [#208](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/208), whose engine no longer reads it, is [#233](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/233)'s, fixed in [PR #256](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/pull/256). Upstream's master has the same code. |
| [#191](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/191) (2026-11-11) | `ql/experimental/barrieroption/suowangdoublebarrierengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/barrier/analyticdoublebarrierengine.{hpp,cpp}` | `SuoWangDoubleBarrierEngine` summed ten images with `std::pow` weights whatever the volatility, as `AnalyticDoubleBarrierEngine` did before #109 and #188. Through the analytic engine's six tests it failed 105 of 150 checks: NaN from 1.5 % volatility down, at zero volatility and with a series of 1,000; 1.8173 where the value is 1.6323 at 1 %; 8.1e-4 for a knock-out worth 5.8e-20 where the volatility is wide; and knock-outs struck beyond the far barrier below zero (−0.139). It now does what the analytic engine does, in the same order: the forward at zero variance, 0 beyond the far barrier, the eigenfunction expansion above σ√T = ln(U/L)/2, and below it Suo and Wang's images with the weights as logs, through the analytic engine's weighted difference, both helpers now `detail::` in its header. It passes all 150, and on #188's grid of 3,456 its worst error below the switch is 6.2e-14, against 2.2e-6, with no negative value. The analytic engine's values are bit-identical. A price costs 57,200–58,800 instructions below the switch and 42,100–44,100 above, against 70,200–73,700. The rebate, which neither engine priced, and the `"rebateIn"` result ([#210](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/210)), and `VannaVolgaDoubleBarrierEngine`'s own no-touch sum ([#192](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/192)), were left, and fixed later. |
| [#192](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/192) (2026-11-11) | `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgadoublebarrierengine.hpp`, `ql/pricingengines/barrier/analyticdoublebarrierbinaryengine.{hpp,cpp}` | `VannaVolgaDoubleBarrierEngine` weighted its smile adjustment by a no-touch probability, its `"lambda"` result, summed over ten images of the spot in the barriers, which left out most of it where σ√T is wide against ln(U/L): on 95–105 over a year, 8.17e-6 where it's 3.48e-9 at 20 % and 1.05e-3 where it's 7.05e-20 at 30 %. With more terms the weights overflow (NaN at 80–120, 10 %). It now takes the probability, still to the quotes' maturity, from the function `AnalyticDoubleBarrierBinaryEngine` prices Double Knock options with, now `detail::doubleBarrierNoTouchProbability` in its header, and agrees with references to 50 digits within 8.8e-14 relative. The binary engine's values are bit-identical, and the engine's test prices move by at most 2.8e-17. The two horizons the engine mixes ([#211](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/211)) are left. Its smile's forward ([#212](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/212)) was left, and fixed later. |
| [#171](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/171) (2026-11-11) | `ql/time/calendars/saudiarabia.{hpp,cpp}` | `SaudiArabia` gives Thursdays a session from May 28th, 1994 to June 14th, 2006, by the CMA's history of the market and its notice of June 2006, where it closed every Thursday until 2013. National Day closes from 2005, when it became a public holiday, and September 24th, 2005 and the four Thursdays of the 2006 share split close. The Eid holidays of 2002 to 2007 follow the sessions in the one daily series that reaches those years, rather than Eid-1 to Eid+4. Only Eid Al-Fitr of 2005 has a second source, and the series has no other day without trades. The calendar equals the series on every day from December 31st, 2001 to December 31st, 2007, 303 sessions in 2002 where it had 251. The twelve breaks that rest on the series alone, and the rule before 2002, are [#214](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/214). |
| [#170](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/170) (2026-11-11) | `ql/time/calendars/israel.{hpp,cpp}` | `Israel::TASE` and `Israel::Settlement`, the default, close on the Knesset elections of 2003, 2006, 2009, 2013 and 2015 and the municipal elections of 2018, where they closed on election days only from 2019. Basic Law: The Knesset makes election day a day of rest, municipal elections have been days off since 2018, and a daily series of the exchange's TA-125 index has no session on any of the six. Against that series, on the 5,217 Mondays to Thursdays from 2000 to 2024, the exchange's calendar then differed on 23 days: five that [#215](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/215) fixes, two that [#216](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/216) does, and 16 that [#218](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/218) keeps, with the Sundays the series can't see. TASE's release for the third quarter of 2026, 61 trading days, confirms the rule for a Friday before a closed Sunday, which had rested on one case, and the test takes that quarter from it. |
| [#215](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/215) (2026-11-11) | `ql/time/calendars/israel.{hpp,cpp}` | `Israel`'s tables had the Jewish New Year of 2001 on September 17th, where Rosh Hashana 5762 fell on the 18th, so all of 2001's Tishrei holidays were a day early in all three markets, and Independence Day of 2038 on May 10th, where Iyar 5th on a Monday moves it to the Tuesday, the 11th, and Memorial Day with it. Both entries move; a Hebrew calendar computed by Reingold and Dershowitz's arithmetic checks every entry of the six tables from 2000 to 2050, and these were the only differences. Upstream has both. The class documentation now gives the tables' range as 2000 to 2050 and Memorial Day as the day before Independence Day, not Nisan 27th. |
| [#216](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/216) (2026-11-11) | `ql/time/calendars/israel.cpp` | `Israel::TASE` closes on February 8th and 9th, 2012, when the Histadrut's general strike closed the exchange (Reuters; Globes; no session in the TA-125 series). Settlement and Sunday the 12th stay open, for want of a source; [#218](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/218) keeps both. |
| [#217](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/217) (2026-11-11) | `ql/time/calendars/israel.cpp` | `Israel::SHIR` closes on election days, as `TASE` and `Settlement` do, where it had only two by date, one of them commented "no idea why", and missed October 27th, 2026. It also closes Easter Monday and December 28th, 2026. The Bank of Israel's lists of days without a SHIR fixing now equal the calendar from 2024 to 2026; the one for 2027 has no holidays abroad yet. |
| [#210](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/210) (2026-11-12) | `ql/pricingengines/barrier/analyticdoublebarrier{,binary}engine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/experimental/barrieroption/suowangdoublebarrierengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgadoublebarrierengine.hpp`, `ql/instruments/doublebarrieroption.hpp` | `AnalyticDoubleBarrierEngine` and `SuoWangDoubleBarrierEngine` left `DoubleBarrierOption`'s rebate out of their values, silently, and `VannaVolgaDoubleBarrierEngine` with them: the issue's knock-out call with a rebate of 5 came out at 1.114682 where the lattice gives 4.187953. They now pay it as the lattice and Monte Carlo engines do, a knock-out's at the first touch of either barrier and a knock-in's at expiry if neither is touched, through `detail::doubleBarrierRebate`, from `AnalyticDoubleBarrierBinaryEngine`'s KIKO and KOKI sums and its no-touch probability: 4.186872 against the lattice's 4.187953 at 2,000 steps, and at r = 0 a knock-out's part and a knock-in's add up to the rebate to 5.9e-16. Without a rebate, values are bitwise the same. The Vanna/Volga engine adds the rebate at the ATM volatility, outside its smile adjustment, as `"RebatePrice"`. Suo-Wang's `"rebateIn"`, which wasn't a rebate's value, is gone, `"rebate"` is the rebate's part, and a price costs 35,100 instructions against 51,800. `DoubleBarrierOption::impliedVolatility` now inverts a value with the rebate, which needn't be monotone in the volatility; its documentation says so. `AnalyticDoubleBarrierBinaryEngine`'s own rebate ([#220](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/220)) was left, and fixed later. |
| [#213](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/213) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgabarrierengine.cpp` | `VannaVolgaBarrierEngine` weights its smile adjustment by the probability at the ATM volatility that the barrier isn't touched, its `"lambda"`. With the drift toward the barrier at low volatility, its `(B/S)^(2 mu / vol^2) N(y)` was overflow times underflow, so `"lambda"` was NaN, and the caps at `[0, vanilla]` turned the NaN price into the vanilla. An up-and-out call at 100 with the barrier at 102, r 5 %, on a flat smile at 0.1 %, came out at the whole vanilla, 4.87705755, where it's worth 0, and its knock-in at 0. Just above the overflow, `N(y)` came out as 0 under a finite power, and lambda lost up to 0.0107. The product now goes through `CumulativeNormalDistribution::logValue()` there, as #109's engines do. On a flat smile the engine gives `AnalyticBarrierEngine`'s values down to 1e-5, and lambda is within 1.4e-13 of 60-digit references down to 0.1 %. Elsewhere, on the release build, values are bitwise the same. The engine's rebate ([#221](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/221)) and its smile interpolation at a zero ATM volatility ([#222](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/222)) were left, and fixed later. |
| [#212](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/212) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgadoublebarrierengine.hpp` | `VannaVolgaDoubleBarrierEngine` read its smile at the strike with the spot as the forward: it passed `VannaVolga` the foreign discount twice, where `VannaVolgaBarrierEngine` passes the domestic and the foreign. So `"VanillaPrice"`, which caps the knock-out, makes the knock-in and enters the price under `adaptVanDelta`, was wrong away from the pillars: on spot 100, r 5 %, a year, the put at 80 came out at 0.339622 where the smile gives 0.190856. It now passes the domestic discount, and its vanilla equals the single-barrier engine's, and a 60-digit evaluation of Castagna and Mercurio's method, within the implied volatility's accuracy. `testVannaVolgaDoubleBarrierValues`' expected values were the engine's own output with the spot as the forward; the five the fix moves past their last decimal are re-derived, each by lambda times the change in the vanilla. |
| [#202](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/202) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.{hpp,cpp}` | `AnalyticHestonForwardEuropeanEngine` priced forward-start options at NaN for resets within weeks: its variance density, `propagator()`, multiplied an exponential that had underflowed by a Bessel function that had overflowed, every reset from a day to 14 with sigma 0.2 on a year's option, and to 47 with sigma 0.11. The density is now taken in logs with the exponentially weighted Bessel function, within 2.9e-14 of Boost's noncentral chi-squared density. Its outer rule, 128 nodes on [0, at least 2], also put a density 0.002 wide between its nodes, and above a variance of 2 could leave it out (77.5 % low, without a NaN). The range is now the variance's mean less 20 standard deviations, floored at 0, to the mean plus 20, keeping the old upper end where it lies 10 to 20 above the mean and reaching at least 10. Against a Gauss-Kronrod reference, all 13 resets sampled from a day to 182 on the issue's two sets price within 2e-7, the residue of an inner 1e-8 node shift (#223); Monte Carlo with 400,000 paths agrees within 0.7 standard errors. Where the Feller condition fails the density is singular at 0 and the rule still couldn't integrate it (#224), and a reset days before expiry lost up to 43 % at the inner cut phi = 100 (#225), before and after; both, and the node shift, were fixed later. |
| [#122](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/122) (2026-11-12) | `ql/time/daycounters/business252.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/time/calendar.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/time/calendars/bespokecalendar.cpp` | `Business252` kept its figures for whole months and years by calendar name, so a count across months ignored a holiday added or removed after first use, and took another calendar's figures under the same name: 109 business days against the calendar's 108, and 113 against 130, from 15 January to 15 June 2030, with no error. The figures are now kept per calendar implementation, each entry holding a copy of its calendar, at most eight per thread, and dropped when any calendar's holidays or weekend change: `Calendar` counts those changes (`detail::calendarChangeCount()`, bumped by `addHoliday`, `removeHoliday`, `resetAddedAndRemovedHolidays` and `BespokeCalendar::addWeekend`), globally, so that a joint calendar's components count too. Counts cost 1.3 % to 3.5 % fewer instructions; a calendar built separately no longer shares figures by name, so one built per instrument fills its own (1.42 million instructions per ten-year count on a new joint calendar each time, against 21,000; #226). [#226](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/226)'s row records its fix. |
| [#206](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/206) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/credit/{integralntdengine,integralcdoengine,midpointcdoengine}.{hpp,cpp}` | `IntegralNtdEngine`, `IntegralCDOEngine` and `MidPointCDOEngine` took the upfront as paid on the first coupon's accrual start, and discounted it from there until that coupon was paid. So inside the first coupon, on a discount curve as of today, all three threw "negative time", whatever the upfront. `IntegralNtdEngine` also took it on the basket's remaining notional: 1 % on a contract of 100 on eight names of 100 came to 7.9989, not 0.99986. Now the upfront doesn't count if it falls before the discount curve's reference date, as `CDO`'s doesn't, which changes nothing that priced, and the NTD's is on the contract's notional. On a discount curve dated on or before the accrual start, an upfront already paid still counts until the first coupon is paid (#227). On default curves as of today the CDO engines still throw inside the first coupon, where their protection starts (#228). [#227](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/227)'s and [#228](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/228)'s rows record their fixes. |
| [#229](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/229) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/math/multidimquadrature.hpp` | `GaussianQuadMultidimIntegrator`'s vector integration multiplied its first node's term by the weight with `std::for_each`, which discards the products. So the node at -6.164, the latent factor's most adverse value at the latent models' order 25, counted with weight 1 instead of 0.8624, at every level. That moved what the latent models integrate as vectors: the binomial and recursive loss models' loss distributions, percentiles and expected shortfalls, and the saddle point's VaR split. On 100 names at correlation 0.3 the Gaussian copula's distribution held 1 + 3.07e-10 of mass, and a Student t copula with 3 degrees moved its 99 % expected shortfall by 1.35e-4 (4793.2926 against 4793.9407). Now the first term is weighted, so each component equals its scalar integral. Premiums and expected tranche losses integrate scalars and didn't move. Gauss-Hermite's own shortfall on t factors is #230. Upstream's master has the same line. |
| [#208](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/208) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/credit/{integralntdengine,basket}.{hpp,cpp}`, `{defaultlossmodel,defaultprobabilitylatentmodel,constantlosslatentmodel}.hpp` | `IntegralNtdEngine` priced every trigger at the recovery of the pool's first name, even once it had defaulted: its `basketIsHomogeneous` was hard-coded to true, and the other branch was dead and couldn't have worked. On eight names with rising recoveries (20 % + 5 % i), the protection was 35-40 % above a Monte Carlo of the same copula at ranks 1 to 3. Now each step pays the claim on the name that triggers it: at the names left's shared recovery where they share one, and otherwise by each name's probability of being the nth default in the step, `DefaultLossModel::probsBeingNthEventBetween`, a new statistic that `DefaultLatentModel` gives exactly in total and to second order in the step among the names. The engine now agrees with the Monte Carlo to 0.23 %. Where recoveries differ, a model without the statistic throws, where it priced at the first name's: `ConstantLossModel` on Trapezoid, and `RandomDefaultLM`, whose own statistic could serve, until [#231](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/231)'s fix ([PR #256](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/pull/256)). There a price costs 13-17 % more at ranks 1 and 3, and 64-70 % more at rank 8. The new virtual moves `DefaultLossModel`'s later vtable slots under the same soname, so whatever was compiled against the old header has to be rebuilt. |
| [#209](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/209) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/credit/randomdefaultlatentmodel.hpp` | `RandomLM::splitVaRAndError`, behind `Basket::splitVaRLevel` on `RandomDefaultLM` and `RandomLossLM`, sorted a path's defaults by the day alone with `std::sort`, so names defaulting on the same day came in an order the standard leaves unspecified, and where the attachment or detachment fell among their losses that order decided which of them took the loss across it: on 20 names at correlation 0.9, by up to 2.5-5.0 % of a name's split between ascending and descending order. Now a day's names take what their defaults add to the tranche's loss averaged over their orders, each one's Shapley value from the portfolio's loss before that day (`detail::sameDayTrancheLosses`), as #162 shares the nth default among a day's names: by the day's subsets up to 16 names whose losses differ, and in proportion to the losses past that. A lone default takes its increment as before. A split costs 2.4-2.5 % more instructions, and 3.2 % on `RandomLossLM`, where on the same names with recoveries drawn at a correlation of 0.9 it moved a name's split by up to 1.92 %. |
| [#207](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/207) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/credit/randomdefaultlatentmodel.hpp` | `RandomLM::probAtLeastNEvents` and `probsBeingNthEvent`, behind `Basket`'s, required a date after today, and `IntegralNtdEngine` asks for the first at the start of each coupon's protection, which is today inside a coupon on a discount curve as of today, so no nth to default priced on `RandomDefaultLM` once its first coupon had begun. On and before today they now give 0 for one default or more, as the paths, whose days the statistics read from today, have none there. On default curves dated today the engine then gives `ConstantLossModel`'s values within 0.65 of a standard error. On default curves dated before today, where it threw, it now prices with [#232](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/232)'s shift: on curves 62 days old, 4.4 % above `ConstantLossModel`'s protection at rank 1, and 1.6 % and 4.6 % below at ranks 2 and 3. Since [#232](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/232)'s fix ([PR #252](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/pull/252)) the statistics count from the earliest curve date, so the early return is at that date, and on such curves the engine gives `ConstantLossModel`'s values within its Monte Carlo error. `RandomLossLM` answers at today too, but still can't price an nth to default; since [#234](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/234) it says why, and the full fix is [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250). [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250)'s row records its fix. |
| [#127](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/127) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/coupons/proxyibor.hpp` | Documentation only: `ProxyIbor`'s spread multiplies. The index forecasts the gearing times the proxied index's fixing times the spread, as it has since the class came in (`5c75b3a88`, 2010, whose message gives that formula), so a spread of 1 means none and 0 gives a rate of 0, where the name suggests an addition. The owner chose to keep the formula and document it. As for any index, a fixing stored under the proxy's own name comes first, and a past fixing date needs one; where the formula applies, the proxied index's stored fixing for today enters it. `testProxyIborFixing` pins each of these. |
| [#195](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/195) (2026-11-12) | `ql/pricingengines/bacheliercalculator.hpp` | `BachelierCalculator::itmAssetProbability` returned the cash probability, N(d) for a call, where its documentation promised the probability of being in the money in the asset measure, which `BlackCalculator`'s gives for a call. A normal forward can be negative, so there is no such measure; the owner chose that quantity anyway, the asset-or-nothing value over the discounted forward: N(d) + s n(d)/F for a call and N(-d) - s n(d)/F for a put. On #195's case (spot 98, r 3 %, q 1 %, a normal volatility of 20, strike 105, a year) it's 0.478232, where it was 0.400902. A put gets its own, as `itmCashProbability` already gave each type its own; `BlackCalculator` gave a put the call's ([#235](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/235), whose row records its fix). It can lie outside [0, 1], 8.4888 for a call at F 1, K 0 and s 20, and the documentation says so; at a forward of 0 it throws. At zero volatility it's the cash probability, as before. `testBachelierCalculatorGreeks`' expected value, from upstream, was the cash probability and changes. |
| [#225](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/225) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.{hpp,cpp}` | `AnalyticHestonForwardEuropeanEngine` took P1 and P2 by Gil-Pelaez on [0, 100], with 128 Gauss-Legendre nodes whatever the tenor from the reset to expiry, so near expiry most of the characteristic function lay beyond the range: a call at the money a day before expiry was 31.2 % low (v0 = theta = 0.04, kappa 2, sigma 0.2, rho -0.7), and the test suite's set was 3 % to 43 % low from two weeks to a day before expiry. P1 and P2 are now taken on the line on which `AnalyticHestonEngine`'s AngledContour control variate prices a call, less a Black-Scholes control variate, out to where the integrand has decayed to 1e-17, in panels of 256 Gauss-Legendre nodes, each twice as wide as the one before and none of more than 64 turns of the integrand. The call above is within 1e-13 of its reference, and a call out of the money five minutes before expiry is worth nothing to 1e-14, where it was -0.268. The rule also ends the inner and outer 1e-8 node shifts (#223's item 3), and `additionalResults["phiRightLimit"]` is gone. At rho = -1 or 1 the characteristic function doesn't decay along the line where it isn't tilted, at the forward and on one side of it, and the engine throws there, where it priced within 6e-7 relative at 182 days before expiry, and 32 % low, or negative, a day before; near rho = -1 or 1 it throws on that side once a node would need more than 192 panels ([#238](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/238)). At rho = -1 and 1 it threw until the row dated 2026-11-16 for #238; just inside them it still refuses where its line isn't tilted ([#303](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/303)). |
| [#224](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/224) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.{hpp,cpp}` | Where the Feller condition fails, R = 4 kappa theta / sigma^2 below 2, the variance's density at the reset is singular at 0, and the engine's 128 Gauss-Legendre nodes couldn't integrate it: with the inner integral right, 12 rows at R from 0.016 to 2.6 were 2.5e-6 to 14.8 % off, and a call at the forward at R 1.6e-4, over five minutes half a year away, was 87 % low before this work. Where the range starts at 0 and R is below 8 the density is now integrated in the square root of the variance, by Gauss-Jacobi nodes for its power there, and the range reaches where at most 1e-14 of the density's mass lies beyond it, by a Chernoff bound. The 12 rows are within 1.1e-13 of their references, and the five-minute row within 4.5e-9, the outer rule's limit there. kappaHat = kappa - rho sigma = 0, where the engine threw "-nan: invalid value", now prices (#223's item 2); R = 0 still throws, now saying why. |
| [#223](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/223), [#237](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/237) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.{hpp,cpp}` | The density came from its Bessel function: at v0 = 0 that is 0/0, NaN at every reset (#223's item 1), and at long resets the Bessel function underflows where the density is of order 1, so a call worth 9.524 at R 138.9 and a five-year reset priced at 0 (#237). It is now summed from the Bessel function's power series wherever the series' terms fall from the first, within 2.6e-12 of Boost's noncentral chi-squared density over 72,000 points. Where R is in the hundreds the Bessel function still underflows over the density's bulk in a band of resets, where the engine priced up to 100 % low: it now refuses a density that is 0 at the variance's mean, or whose weights over the outer nodes miss 1 by more than 1e-8, which at R 826 refuses resets from 184 to 227 days. #237 stayed open for that band, which the row dated 2026-11-15 for #237 covers. |
| [#223](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/223) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.{hpp,cpp}` | The constructor refused a vol of vol at or below 0.1, which nothing had measured (#223's item 6). With the density's series the engine prices sigma from 0.1 down to 0.01 within 1.4e-13 of the reference, and the density guard refuses the band of resets where its Bessel function underflows, so the constructor now requires only sigma > 0. Below a sigma of 1e-6 the inner integral takes the characteristic function's log as `AnalyticHestonEngine::chF` takes it. |
| [#223](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/223) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.{hpp,cpp}` | Resets within 1e-3 years were priced as vanilla options from v0, which leaves out how the variance moves until the reset (#223's item 5). On the reset date that's exact, and only high-resolution dates reach the rest, where the price moved with the reset time at -2.61 a year where it moves at -3.02; at v0 = 0 every one threw. The fallback now covers resets within 1e-11 years, where the integral's error and the fallback's are both near 2e-11, and the integral prices the rest. |
| [#223](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/223) (2026-11-12) | `test-suite/forwardoption.cpp` | Test only. `testHestonAnalyticalVsMCPrices` incremented its moneyness index twice, so it never tested moneyness 1.0 and checked 1.2 against 1.0's tolerances (#223's item 4). It now tests all three against their own. |
| [#221](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/221) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgabarrierengine.{hpp,cpp}` | `VannaVolgaBarrierEngine` priced its knock-out with the rebate, capped it at the vanilla, and priced a knock-in as the vanilla less that, so a knock-in lost the knock-out's rebate and never took its own, and a knock-out its rebate lifted above the vanilla was capped. On a flat smile, where it should give `AnalyticBarrierEngine`'s values, a call at 100 with a rebate of 3 came out at 8.0665 for the up-and-in against 10.9505, 0 for the down-and-in against 2.3084, and 10.4506 for the down-and-out against 11.0247. As [#210](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/210) did for the double-barrier engine, it now prices the knock-out without the rebate, through the smile adjustment and the caps, and adds the rebate at the ATM volatility to the exercise date, as `"RebatePrice"`; the smile adjustment covers the option only. On a flat smile it equals `AnalyticBarrierEngine` to 8.9e-16; without a rebate, values are bitwise the same. Uncapped knock-outs with a rebate move on a smile, by 1.3 % to 1.8 % on a 23/20/21 one, as the rebate is no longer smile-adjusted. The class has a comment now. |
| [#220](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/220) (2026-11-12) | `ql/pricingengines/barrier/analyticdoublebarrierbinaryengine.{hpp,cpp}` | `AnalyticDoubleBarrierBinaryEngine` never read the rebate, so it dropped out of the value silently: a double knock-out paying 10 on 80-120 with a rebate of 5 came out at 3.515519 where the lattice gives 6.587026. It now pays it where `BinomialDoubleBarrierEngine` does: a knock-out's and a knock-in's through #210's `detail::doubleBarrierRebate`, a KIKO's as what the engine pays at a hit for a KOKI plus the rebate at expiry times the probability of no touch, and a KOKI's the same with the KIKO's. An option knocked out by a spot already at or past a barrier is worth its rebate, paid at once, where it was worth 0. The rebate's parts agree with an independent Crank-Nicolson solution to 9.6e-9 on 56 cases; without a rebate, values are bitwise the same. |
| [#222](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/222) (2026-11-12) | `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgainterpolation.hpp` | `VannaVolgaInterpolation`'s `update()` appended to its premia and vegas without clearing them, so after a second `update()` it paired the new ATM volatility with the old quotes' premia: 0.307915 at 100 where a fresh interpolation gives 0.307513. It clears them now. At a zero ATM volatility, with two equal strikes, or with a quote too many ATM standard deviations from the forward for its vega (about 38), it divided 0 by 0 and threw "option price (nan) must be non-negative"; it now says which input fails, when it's built. Its precondition says distinct strikes, where it said sorted: the engines' 25-delta put strike can lie above the ATM one. Finite values are unchanged. Its overshoot between quotes a few ATM standard deviations apart is [#242](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/242), whose row records what changed: the class doc says where the method holds, and the overshoot stays. |
| [#241](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/241) (2026-11-12) | `ql/pricingengines/barrier/analyticbarrierengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/barrier/discretizedbarrieroption.cpp`, `ql/experimental/barrieroption/discretizeddoublebarrieroption.cpp`, `ql/experimental/barrieroption/vannavolgabarrierengine.hpp` | `AnalyticBarrierEngine` paid a rebate only if it was positive, so a negative one dropped out of the value at any positive volatility, though its zero-variance branch paid it: an up-and-out call struck at 100 with the barrier at 120 and a rebate of -3 came out at 1.17607, its value without the rebate, where it is worth -0.03192. The Derman-Kani lattices, single and double, floored the value next to a barrier at 0, which clipped a negative rebate: the same option came out at 1.03154 at 2,000 steps, and a double knock-out on 80-120 with a rebate of -5 at 0.96303 where it is worth -1.95751. Both now pay a rebate of either sign, as the plain lattice, the Monte Carlo and finite-difference engines and the double-barrier engines do, and `BarrierOption::impliedVolatility`, the quanto engine on `AnalyticBarrierEngine` and `VannaVolgaBarrierEngine`'s rebate follow. Without a negative rebate, values are bitwise the same. The lattices also floored a European payoff at 0 at expiry, which dropped a gap payoff's negative part: [#243](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/243), whose row records its fix. |
| [#247](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/247) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{randomdefaultlatentmodel,randomlosslatentmodel}.hpp` | `RandomDefaultLM` and `RandomLossLM` found each default's day with Brent as the root of P(t) = p, on a curve read at whole days, which is a step function, and truncated it. So about half of each day's defaults were recorded on the next day, and a statistic at today + D was about 0.5/D low: at a hazard rate of 1, on 8 names, the probability of a default within a day came out at 0.0107 against `ConstantLossModel`'s 0.0210. `detail::defaultDay` now finds the k with P(k) <= p < P(k + 1) by a binary search over whole days, up to the paths' last day, and a simulation takes about a third of the instructions it did. The constructors keep their accuracy argument, unused. `CdoTests`' 40 random-default values in testHW and testHWStudentT rise by 0.002 % to 0.179 %. Upstream's master has the same solver. |
| [#232](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/232) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{randomdefaultlatentmodel,randomlosslatentmodel}.hpp`, `test-suite/nthtodefault.cpp` | The random models' paths count each default's day from its own curve's reference date, as the latent models count a name's probability, but every statistic read the days as counted from today. So on curves older than today every default moved later by the curves' age, and [#207](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/207)'s early return left no default by today: on curves of September 30th and October 15th, with today November 1st, `RandomDefaultLM` gave 0 by today against `ConstantLossModel`'s 0.0954, and 0.114 against 0.192 by December 1st. On curves starting after today it gave defaults before they start, and most statistics moved when today did. Each simulation now counts days from `origin_`, the earliest curve date of the names left, as the statistics do, and #207's early returns move to `d <= origin_`. On those curves both models are within 1.42 standard errors of `ConstantLossModel`, and no statistic moves with today. `testRandomDefaultModelInsideACoupon` dates its curves at the basket's inception. Upstream's master has the same code. |
| [#244](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/244) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{randomlosslatentmodel,randomdefaultlatentmodel}.hpp` | `RandomLossLM` recorded a default's name in 12 bits and its day in 12, unchecked, so a day past 4095 wrapped round to an early one, as it did for names whose curves predated today by more than 45 days, and an index past 4095 wrapped too. The name now takes 11 bits and the day 13, in the same 4 bytes, and each model refuses, as a simulation starts, a basket or curves its records can't hold: `RandomLossLM` over 2048 names or curves over 4141 days before today, `RandomDefaultLM` over 65536 names or curves over 61485 days before today. A basket of 2049 to 4096 names fitted `RandomLossLM` before and is refused now. An 8-byte record would have taken 59 % to 83 % more heap for the paths at 125 names. Upstream's master has the same field. |
| [#245](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/245) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/randomlosslatentmodel.hpp` | `RandomLossLM` stored a recovery as `lround(r * 256)` in 8 bits, so one of 0.998 or more came out as 0, a total loss. It's kept at 255/256 at most now, the nearest to 1 the field holds. Upstream's master has the same field. |
| [#246](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/246) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{randomlosslatentmodel,spotlosslatentmodel}.hpp` | `RandomLossLM` read each default's recovery at the probability of default by the start of its day. That is 0 on a curve's first day, where `SpotRecoveryLatentModel::conditionalRecovery` returned 0, so every default there lost everything: at a hazard rate of 3, on 8 names, the first day's defaults lost 67 % more than their probabilities give at recoveries of 40 %. Each default now takes the recovery at its own probability, from the path's latent default sample, through the new `SpotRecoveryLatentModel::conditionalRecoveryAtDefault`. The date version delegates to it, and refuses a date by which the curve has no default, where it returned 0. `SpotRecoveryLatentModel::expectedLoss`, which didn't compile when called, was left to [#248](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/248). Upstream's master has the same guard. |
| [#233](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/233) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{basket.cpp,basket.hpp,defaultlossmodel.hpp,randomdefaultlatentmodel.hpp}` | `Basket::probsBeingNthEvent` gave one 0 per name left once the defaults before the evaluation date reached n, and the model's vector, one per name in the pool, below that, so its length depended on n: on eight names with one default, 7 entries at n = 1 and 8 at n = 2. It now gives one 0 per name in the pool, as `probsBeingNthEventBetween` does. `Basket`'s doc called n "the internal index to the name", `DefaultLossModel`'s said "(remaining) basket elements", and `RandomLM`'s said its order was the "simulated (super)pool" one; all three say the pool's order now. Upstream's master has the same code. |
| [#231](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/231) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{randomdefaultlatentmodel,integralntdengine}.hpp` | `IntegralNtdEngine` threw on an nth to default on `RandomDefaultLM` wherever the names' recoveries differ, as the model didn't give each name's probability of being the nth default in a step ([#208](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/208)); before #208 it priced every trigger at the first name's recovery, 38.11 at rank 1 where `ConstantLossModel` gives 27.21. `RandomLM::probsBeingNthEventBetween` now gives it from the paths `probsBeingNthEvent` counts, through a helper the two share, so name by name it's the difference of `probsBeingNthEvent` at the period's ends to 1e-15, and `probsBeingNthEvent` gives the same values to the bit. The engine's protection is `ConstantLossModel`'s within the Monte Carlo error: z of -0.47 and -1.16 at ranks 1 and 2 on 100 seeds of 20,000 paths. Its checks mirror `ConstantLossModel`'s. Upstream's master has neither the statistic nor #208's engine change. |
| [#234](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/234) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/{randomlosslatentmodel,integralntdengine}.hpp` | `RandomLossLM` gave no expected recovery, through `DefaultLossModel`'s generic "expected recovery Not implemented for this model.", so `IntegralNtdEngine` threw it on any nth to default. The model draws a recovery with each default, and on its paths the triggers' mean recovery is 0.435, 0.386 and 0.350 at ranks 1 to 3 on eight names, where each name's given its default is 0.400; an expected recovery by name would misprice the protection by +6.1 %, -2.4 % and -7.9 %. So it now refuses with a message that says why, and the engine still throws on it. The path-average protection is [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250), for the owner. Upstream's master has the same code. [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250)'s row records its fix. |
| [#251](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/251) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/randomdefaultlatentmodel.hpp` | `RandomLM::probsBeingNthEvent` required the rank to be at most the pool's size, but `Basket` passes the rank less the defaults so far, and the model counts among the names left. On eight names, rank 9 threw "Impossible number of defaults." with no default so far, and gave eight 0s after one, where `probsBeingNthEventBetween` and `probAtLeastNEvents` gave 0s either way, as `ConstantLossModel` does. Each default now loses at the recovery of its own latent value. On the Gaussian copula that's a bivariate normal probability given the factors, and `expectedLoss` is p (1 - Rbar) within 5e-8. On the T copula they throw, as the model doesn't keep the single-name loss there ([#255](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/255)); the doc says so, and writes the loss p (1 - RR_i) where it had p RR_i. `conditionalExpLossRR` and `expectedLoss` return 0 at a date by which the curve has no default. | [#265](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/265) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/defaultprobabilitylatentmodel.hpp` | `DefaultLatentModel::probAtLeastNEvents` and `probOfDefault` took each live name's probability from its curve and inverted it through the copula at every node of the factor integration, though neither depends on the node, and `probAtLeastNEvents` summed over every set of the L names left, 2^L L products a node. On `TCopulaPolicy` each inversion is a Brent search. Both now invert once a call, as `probsBeingNthEventBetween` and `defaultCorrelation` already did, and a name below 1e-10 still gives 0 at every node; the count goes one name at a time ([#264](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/264)). `NthToDefaultTests`' 16 cases take 13.6e9 instructions where they took 113.0e9. On a probe of 1,110 values each name's probability of default and of being the nth default is the same to the bit, and the probabilities of at least n defaults move by at most 1.1e-15 relatively. `RecursiveLossModel`'s own per-node inversion went to [#261](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/261), whose fix reworks that code. Upstream's master inverts at every node too. |
| [#264](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/264) (2026-11-13) | `ql/experimental/credit/defaultprobabilitylatentmodel.hpp`, `test-suite/nthtodefault.cpp` | `DefaultLatentModel::probAtLeastNEvents` enumerated the sets of names in a 64-bit count up to `static_cast<BigNatural>(std::pow(2., L))`. It now builds the count's distribution one name at a time, in L n steps, sharing the step with `detail::conditionalProbsBeingNthEventBetween`. | [#273](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/273) (2026-11-14) | `ql/experimental/credit/binomiallossmodel.hpp`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | `BinomialLossModel` built the binomial distribution of defaults given the factor by a forward recursion from (1 − p)ⁿ, which underflowed where a default given the factor was nearly certain: on 100 names once 1 − p was below 5.8e-4, on 125 below 2.6e-3, and on 1,000 names once p was above about 0.52. The distribution there came out all zeros but for the 1 − alpha its mean adjustment adds, so the tranche losses counted none of the losses where defaults are likeliest. On names alike, against `RecursiveLossModel` at the same nodes, they were up to 5.9 % low on 100 names at correlation 0.9, 83 % on 1,000, and 10 % on t5 factors at 0.9. The distribution now starts from its mode at 1, recurses both ways and is divided by its sum, in the loop that already scales it by alpha. It agrees within 2.2e-14 on Gaussian factors and 1.8e-10 on t5 ones, where what's left is the t rule's ([#271](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/271)), and `lossDistribution` sums to 1, which settles the code's FIX ME. Where nothing underflowed, values move by rounding: at most 6.2e-14 on the suite's 20 names. Upstream's master has the same code. |
| [#227](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/227) (2026-11-14) | `ql/experimental/credit/{integralntdengine,integralcdoengine,midpointcdoengine}.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/{cdo,nthtodefault}.cpp` | `IntegralNtdEngine`, `IntegralCDOEngine` and `MidPointCDOEngine` take the upfront as paid on the first coupon's accrual start, but counted it until the first coupon was paid and then dropped it with nothing paid. So inside the first coupon, on a discount curve dated on or before its accrual start, an upfront already paid still counted. As the owner decided on 2026-11-13, an upfront now counts only if it's paid on or after today and, as since [#206](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/206), on or after the discount curve's reference date. Paid on the first coupon's accrual start, it no longer counts once that date has passed. Upstream's master counts it until the first coupon is paid, as the fork did. |
| [#228](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/228) (2026-11-14) | `ql/experimental/credit/{integralntdengine,integralcdoengine,midpointcdoengine}.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/{cdo,nthtodefault}.cpp` | Once a coupon had begun, the three engines counted its premium and its protection over different spans. The CDO engines counted every coupon not paid by today in full, and while the first was unpaid they started the protection from the expected tranche loss on its accrual start. So on default curves as of today, which start after that date, both threw "negative time" inside the first coupon, and on default curves dated after it, as with settlement days, they threw at any valuation before the first coupon was paid. Once it was paid they started from the losses realised so far, so on default curves dated before today the expected losses from the curves' date to today counted as protection still to come. `IntegralNtdEngine` counted the whole coupon in its premium leg and only what was left of it in its protection, so on eight names a first to default's fair premium dipped from 0.09078 at the first accrual start to 0.08483 inside the coupon; on a discount curve dated before today it counted coupons already paid. As the owner decided on 2026-11-13, once a coupon has begun the three engines count both legs from today: the premium leg counts the coupon less the premium accrued so far, and the protection starts from the expected tranche loss on the latest of today, the first unpaid coupon's accrual start and the default curves' reference dates. That first to default's premium is now 0.08944. On default curves as of today the 0-10 % tranche prices at 0.16378 (integral) and 0.16482 (mid-point) inside the first coupon, and after the first payment only the premium accrued before today moves, except on the pool models, whose expected tranche loss today wasn't the loss realised so far until [#259](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/259) and [#260](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/260) were fixed: their protection moved by up to 3.6 % on `CdoTests`' 20 names. On default curves from before today the tranche's fair premium after the first payment goes from 0.17773 to 0.16335 (mid-point 0.17888 to 0.16441). `SyntheticCDO::implicitCorrelation` prices on `MidPointCDOEngine` and moves with it. Valuing within the default curves' lag before a coupon's end still threw, as did `IntegralNtdEngine` on default curves dated after a discount curve as of today, until the row dated 2026-11-18 for [#275](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/275), and `IntegralCDOEngine` integrated each coupon to its payment date rather than its accrual end until the row dated 2026-11-18 for [#276](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/276). Upstream's master takes the same spans. |
| [#250](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/250) (2026-11-14) | `ql/experimental/credit/{defaultlossmodel.hpp,basket.hpp,basket.cpp,randomdefaultlatentmodel.hpp,randomlosslatentmodel.hpp,integralntdengine.hpp,integralntdengine.cpp}`, `test-suite/nthtodefault.cpp` | `IntegralNtdEngine` paid the protection at the loss model's expected recovery for the name that triggers it. `RandomLossLM` draws a recovery with each default and, since [#234](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/234), refuses an expected recovery with a message saying why, so the engine threw on any nth to default on it. No expected recovery by name prices it right: on #250's eight names the triggers' mean recoveries are 0.435, 0.386 and 0.350 at ranks 1 to 3, where each name's is 0.400, which would misprice the protection by +6.1 %, -2.4 % and -7.9 %. As the owner decided on 2026-11-13, `DefaultLossModel::recoveriesBeingNthEventBetween` now gives each name's mean recovery given that it's the nth default in a period, and `Basket`'s counts the defaults so far; by default it's each name's expected recovery, which the engine read before. `RandomLossLM` overrides it with the mean of the recoveries the name's defaults drew on the paths where it's the nth default, sharing a day's defaults as `probsBeingNthEventBetween` does ([#209](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/209)), and the engine reads it once a step. On 100,000 paths the engine on daily steps gives the paths' own protection, 27.1129, 14.3888 and 7.1791 at ranks 1 to 3, to 7e-15. Every other model gives the engine the same recoveries at the same dates, and a probe of both random models' statistics and of the engine's legs on four models gives the parent's bytes but on `RandomLossLM`'s contracts. A contract the defaults so far have triggered prices at no protection, where the engine threw. `DefaultLossModel`'s vtable gains an entry, so whatever was compiled against its old header has to be rebuilt. Upstream's master has the same gap: its `RandomLossLM` gives no expected recovery either, so its engine throws there, and it pays every other model's triggers at the pool's first name's recovery ([#208](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/208)). |
| [#280](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/280) (2026-11-14) | `ql/experimental/credit/saddlepointlossmodel.hpp`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | Where a node of the factor makes names certain to default, `SaddlePointLossModel` still took them as uncertain, so a level within rounding of their loss reached the saddle-point search of [#160](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/160)'s fix, where K' only tends to the level: the search stopped on a flat stretch, K'' rounded negative, tail probabilities and densities came out NaN, and `percentile` and `expectedShortfall` threw "root not bracketed". On `cdo.cpp`'s `NamesAroundADefault` pool before any default (20 names losing 60, Gaussian copula, five years out) that hit the 0-6 %, 3-6 % and 6-9 % tranches, and on 20 names that differ, 36 to 45 node-levels a date. Given the factor, the certain names now count as a certain loss, and #160's exact regions apply to what the others lose, tested in amounts with the losses summed with compensation, not in fractions of the notional, where a sum of losses could fall on either side of a level: on four names losing 60, three of them certain at every node, P(L ≥ 180) came out 0.0488 where it is 1. A level a double or two past a region's edge now takes the region's value: on 20 names alike P(L ≥ 1140.0000000000002) goes from 3.62e-8 to 3.82e-10 one year out under the Gaussian copula, and from 7.60e-4 to 3.63e-4 five years out under the T of order 3. One NaN moves: on four names losing 60, the fourth on a loading of √0.999, 180 is now the top of the region searched, and at five nodes it meets [#282](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/282)'s plateau, NaN where the parent gave 0.00479 against the exact 0.0362, until the row dated 2026-11-16 for #282, which takes it exactly. The search is the fork's, from #160's fix; upstream's is different. |
| [#283](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/283) (2026-11-14) | `ql/experimental/credit/saddlepointlossmodel.hpp`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | A name that loses nothing, at a recovery of 1, made the smallest loss one default causes 0 in [#160](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/160)'s exact regions, so `SaddlePointLossModel` lost them for the whole pool: on four names with recoveries of 0.4 but the fourth's 1, P(L ≥ 30) came out 0.2804 and P(L ≥ 60) 0.1073 where both are 0.2344, P(L ≥ 180) 0 where it is 0.0050, the median 10.33 where it is 0, and the 99 % percentile 164.84 where it is 120. Such a name is now left out of the regions, since none of its defaults changes the loss, and those values are exact. The regions are the fork's, from #160's fix. |
| [#226](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/226) (2026-11-15) | `ql/time/daycounters/business252.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/time/calendar.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/time/calendars/{jointcalendar,bespokecalendar}.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/time/calendars/nullcalendar.hpp` | Since [#122](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/122), `Business252` kept its figures for whole months and years per calendar implementation, and `JointCalendar`, `BespokeCalendar` and `NullCalendar` build one for each calendar, so calendars built alike for each instrument didn't share figures, and more than eight of them in turn filled theirs again on every count, at about the cost of counting the days one by one. Any change to any calendar's holidays or weekend dropped every thread's figures too, so building a `BespokeCalendar` and setting its weekend dropped them. Calendars now share figures when their keys are equal: `Calendar::Impl::appendBusinessDaysKey`, new and virtual, appends what an implementation's business days depend on (by default its address, so named calendars are told apart as before; `JointCalendar` its rule and its calendars' keys, `NullCalendar` a tag, `BespokeCalendar` its weekend), and `detail::appendBusinessDaysKey` then appends the calendar's added and removed holidays. `addHoliday`, `removeHoliday`, `resetAddedAndRemovedHolidays` and `BespokeCalendar::addWeekend` make `Business252` check a calendar's key again before using its figures, so the calendars a change doesn't touch keep theirs; `detail::recordCalendarChange()`, for changes keys don't show, still drops them all. A ten-year count on a joint calendar built for each count costs 18,744 instructions, against 1.42 million, and one on `Brazil()` reused 11,416, against 11,375. `Calendar::Impl`'s vtable gains an entry, so whatever was compiled against its old header has to be rebuilt. An implementation whose business days depend on another calendar must now append that calendar's key, as `JointCalendar`'s does, or its figures stay those of the other calendar's earlier holidays; under #122's drop-all it needed nothing. |
| [#203](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/203) (2026-11-15) | `ql/experimental/credit/distribution.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/experimental/credit/{homogeneouspooldef,inhomogeneouspooldef}.hpp`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | `Distribution::expectedShortfall` summed from the bucket after the one holding the level, wherever the level fell in it, weighted each bucket by its lower edge where `confidenceLevel` returns upper edges, and divided by less than the probability it summed. `HomogeneousPoolLossModel` and `InhomogeneousPoolLossModel` took a tranche's shortfall from it on the tranched distribution, so on #159's pool it was a step function of the level (129.39 at both 0.3 and 0.5 on 0-20 %) and, at the top, a bucket below the percentile (396 where the percentile is 400). It is now the mean of `confidenceLevel` over the levels above. The pool models take a tranche's from the new `Distribution::trancheExpectedShortfall`, the mean over the same levels of the tranche's loss at `confidenceLevel`, so theirs is the mean of their percentile, as on the other loss models since #159. On the same class, `cumulativeDensity` grows from 0 at the first bucket's lower edge, `locate` puts a value within rounding below the first edge in the first bucket, and `tranche` gives each bucket it keeps its density, excess probability and cumulative excess probability from the cumulative probabilities, where the last bucket's density and excess probability were 0 and the cumulative excess probabilities weren't kept. Nothing in `ql/` calls `tranche()` any more. On the evaluation date the pool models' shortfall isn't 0, as their percentile isn't (#288), and the mass their factor rule leaves out counts as a loss beyond every bucket, on t factors 1.3e-3 to 3.2e-3 of it until #259's fix. |
| [#235](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/235) (2026-11-15) | `ql/pricingengines/blackcalculator.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/bacheliercalculator.hpp`, `ql/instruments/oneassetoption.hpp`, `test-suite/blackcalculator.cpp` | `BlackCalculator::itmCashProbability()` and `itmAssetProbability()` returned N(d2) and N(d1) for a put as for a call, so a put got its probability of ending out of the money, and `AnalyticEuropeanEngine`, and `BaroneAdesiWhaleyApproximationEngine` and `JuQuadraticApproximationEngine` where early exercise is never optimal, reported it as the put's `itmCashProbability()`: on #235's market (spot 100, strike 105, 20 %, a year, r 3 %, q 0) a European put's was 0.423107, where it is 0.576893. Each type now gets its own, a put N(-d2) and N(-d1), as `blackFormulaCashItmProbability`, `blackFormulaAssetItmProbability` and `BachelierCalculator` already gave. A put's are computed from d2 and d1 themselves, not as 1 - N(d2), so that a small one isn't lost to cancellation. A call's are unchanged. A caller that took 1 less a put's value to get the put's probability now gets the call's; nothing in the library, its tests or its examples did. `BlackCalculator`, and so `BlackScholesCalculator`, gains a member (160 bytes become 168, and 176 become 184), so code compiled against the old header has to be rebuilt. |

Outside that its values leave the quotes' range with no warning, and further out its call price leaves the bounds a call can take, where the implied volatility threw "option price (-5.95206e+31) must be non-negative", "negative Put price (-1.66355) implied by put-call parity" or "root not bracketed". The class doc now says where it holds, measured over 18,040 markets (three months to ten years, ATM volatilities of 1 % to 40 %, 25-delta volatilities 0.7 to 4 times the ATM one): with the higher 25-delta volatility at most twice the ATM one and its total volatility below 1, none of the 1,118 markets with both at least the ATM one threw; with one below it, the values past that quote keep falling, and most markets fail there unless the other lies far enough above the ATM volatility (the lower at least 0.97 to 0.75 times the ATM one as the higher goes from 1.05 to 2 times it). So ordinary skewed smiles fail, as they did before: ATM 10 %, the 25-delta put at 12.5 % and the call at 8.7 %, a year, from a strike of 122.5 ([#291](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/291)). Where the price leaves a call's bounds, `value()` now says that the method doesn't hold for the smile, with the price, the bounds and where its quotes lie; any other failure keeps its message. Both Vanna/Volga engines' docs point to the class doc. No value changes, to rounding: without floating-point contraction the values are bitwise the same, and with GCC's fused multiply-adds 47 of 10.9 million strikes differ, by up to 5.8e-14 relative. Refusing smiles beyond a set distance wasn't taken: a cut at 2 ATM standard deviations would refuse 3,979 of 17,262 markets and let 5,346 bad ones through. |

Where the model takes the distribution given the factor exactly, below the two smallest losses of one default together and above the total less those two ([#160](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/160), [#205](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/205), [#282](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/282)), it is a step function, and wherever 1 - p lay inside a jump there was no root: Brent stopped anywhere within its accuracy of the loss the jump is at, on either side. At the top that put the percentile above the most the tranche can lose, and `expectedShortfall`, whose excess beyond the top is 0, returned the percentile itself, so on `NamesAroundADefault`'s 19 names left after its default, under T of order 3, the shortfall of 50 to 100 % fell as the level rose; at a probability of 1 the percentile was the whole tranche, 1,900 and 950, where the pool can lose 1,140. The percentile now takes the loss the jump is at: at a probability of 1, and where 1 - p is at most P at the total less half the smallest loss, the most the tranche can lose, what the names that can default by the date lose less the attachment (`mostTrancheLoss`); elsewhere, after solving, the first atom of the exact regions within twice the solver's accuracy of the solution at which the model's own P jumps across 1 - p. Neither the percentile nor the shortfall is above that most. At 432 levels inside jumps on `NamesAroundADefault`'s names, alike and differing, under both copulas, the percentile is on the atom at all of them, where it was at none and off by up to 0.324, and the shortfall on 0 to 100 % and 50 to 100 % is the exact one to the 12 digits printed, where it was off by up to 4.79. Where names certain to default given the factor moved the jumps off the atoms the percentile tried, it was still solved until [#317](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/317)'s fix ([PR #321](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/pull/321)). |

The regions then ended and started off the losses the others make: on four names that lose 60, 70, 80 and 90 beside one at a hazard rate of 0 that would lose 200, the region at the top started at 370 rather than 170, P(L ≥ 241) was 0.0132 against an exact 0.0073, the density -1.14 at 299, where the exact one is 0, and the percentile at 0.99 251.68 against 240. The loss limits and the two exact tails now share one predicate, `uncertainName`, under which such a name is neither certain nor uncertain; it adds nothing to the cumulant generating function, the expected loss or the probability that none defaults, so only the regions move. At 748 levels and 70 percentiles with their shortfalls on seven pools with such names, 179 rows were off exact, and none is now. [#307](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/307)'s check at the top now runs at every node, as its level lies in an exact region there. There its density summed K'', K''' and K'''' name by name from powers of p e^{ls}, which overflowed far out in the tilt and lost their digits where a name's tilted probability is near 1, and where one name decides the level the second-order factor F is negative however precisely it is taken: over twelve pools, under Gaussian and t3 factors, the density given the factor was negative at 8,923 points of the band and 157 at its top, and not finite at 598. A name that loses more than the two smallest together puts two atoms between e2 and e3, its own loss and the total less it, which the approximation smeared: on #308's plateau, three names that lose 60 beside one that loses 150 on a loading of sqrt(0.999), P(L ≥ 180) was 0.0190475 a year out against an exact 0.0173413, and 0.12869 by 2011 against 0.124836. Past four, the node keeps the approximation ([#325](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/325)). Next to an atom that names all but certain make under the tilt, the density still rises like the inverse square root of the distance ([#324](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/324)), and where the others need the approximation P can step across their joins with their exact regions ([#309](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/309)). Against `RecursiveLossModel` with a loss unit that divides every loss, which is exact given the factor, on the model's own factor rule, on 112 rows (Gaussian at 0.3 and T of order 3, one and five years, seven tranches), it was off by up to 105 % on 10 names alike, 42 % on NamesAroundADefault's 20 and 26 % on 100 names alike.
