On the 112 rows it is within 4.2 % on 10 names alike, and exact on every tranche below two defaults' loss, within 1.29 % on 100 names alike and within 3.3 % on NamesAroundADefault's 20; it is closer to the exact value than the old on 95 rows, as close on 16, and further on one, by 1.91 % against 1.38 %. Elsewhere the tail comes from the first-order approximation, which is off just above a lattice's atoms ([#309](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/309)) and smears the atoms of more than four such names ([#325](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/325)): on six names that each lose 150 beside two that lose 60, the tranche from 9 % to 12 % comes out 20 % to 91 % above the exact one, where the old value was 4.6 % to 57 % below. The owner took it on 2026-11-18 with two parts of the acceptance rule set of 2026-11-14 set aside: rule 3, within 3 % of the exact value (15 % on 10 names) and no row further than the old value, and rule 5, at most three times the old cost. | [#259](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/259) (2026-11-18) | `ql/experimental/credit/{homogeneouspooldef,inhomogeneouspooldef}.hpp`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | `HomogeneousPoolLossModel` and `InhomogeneousPoolLossModel` integrate their one factor by the midpoint rule on [min, max] in nSteps steps. The rule was in the factor itself, so on a t factor [−5, 5] left out 3.2e-3 of a t3 factor's mass and 1.3e-3 of a t5 one's, and the expected tranche loss counted it as a loss beyond every bucket: on ten names alike at hazard 1 %, the whole pool's expected loss by a year came out 41 % high on t3 factors and 18 % on t5 ones, at 12,000 buckets on the default rule. The rule is now in the factor's normal quantile u, as the latent models' rule for t factors has been since [#230](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/230): the node at u is the factor's value F^-1(Phi(u)), and its weight the step times phi(u). Gaussian values keep their bytes, as do those of any copula policy but the t one, and nodes past \|u\| of about 37.5, where Phi(-\|u\|) underflows, are skipped. A step in u spans more of a t factor in its tails than in its middle, so few steps resolve a thin tranche at a short horizon worse than the rule in the factor did: on t3 factors at correlation 0.3 and 15 steps, 100 names' 6–10 % tranche by a year comes out 53.7 % high, where the old rule gave −3.3 % by cancelling its truncation against its coarseness. `testHWStudentT`'s t data sets now take 50 steps. The percentile and the shortfall still count what the rule leaves out as a loss beyond every bucket: 5.7e-7 of any factor's mass on [−5, 5] in u, and 1.9e-17 on the default rule. |

They now integrate the tranche's expected loss given the factor, node by node over the factor's rule. On the homogeneous model that is the binomial loss's, exact given the factor. The inhomogeneous model takes each bucket's losses at their average, which is exact where each bucket holds losses of one value; on the twelve names it is off by up to 3.6e-3 at 50 buckets and 2.8e-5 at 200 ([#323](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/323)). No loss counts up to the top of its bucket now, and the mass the rule leaves out counts as none, so on default curves as of today the expected tranche loss today is 0: on ten names at 101 buckets the bucket-top counting gave a tranche attached at 0 a bucket's width, and the mass the rule left out gave a 3–6 % tranche 1.6e-5 on a Gaussian factor and 0.096 on t3 factors. `testHW`'s and `testHWStudentT`'s rows move by up to 4.9 bp and all pass. Only `expectedTrancheLoss` changes, and `Distribution::cumulativeExcessProbability`, which it used, has no caller in the library now. |

| [#276](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/276) (2026-11-18) | `ql/experimental/credit/integralcdoengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/experimental/credit/midpointcdoengine.hpp`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | `IntegralCDOEngine` stepped each coupon from the later of its accrual start and today to its payment date, and discounted each step's premium from the step's end. `SyntheticCDO` pays its coupons on dates its payment convention adjusts, so a payment can come after its coupon's accrual end or before it. Where it came after, the days between counted in that coupon and again in the next, and after the last coupon the protection ran past maturity; where it came before, as under Preceding payments, they counted in neither; and wherever a coupon took more than one step its premium came out high. On #276's example, eight names and their 0-10 % tranche on the Gaussian LHP model at a correlation of 0.3, on quarterly unadjusted dates, the premium leg on Following payments was 12.177383, 0.59 % above its 12.105974 on unadjusted payments, and on unadjusted payments it was 0.43 %, 0.59 % and 0.63 % high at steps of a month, a week and a day. The engine now follows `MidPointCDOEngine`'s rule: a coupon's steps run to its accrual end, and each step's premium is discounted from the coupon's payment date. That premium leg is now 12.105067, where the mid-point engine gives 12.105067083, and the expected tranche losses the engine reports are at the coupons' accrual ends, where the mid-point engine takes them. Each step's losses are still discounted from the step's end, but from no earlier than the discount curve's reference date. `testHW`'s 68 integral rows rise by 2.20e-5 to 2.77e-5, relatively, from two coupons longer than three months, and no tolerance changes. The integral engine's figures in the row for [#228](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/228) move in their fifth digit: 0.16378 becomes 0.16379, and 0.16335 becomes 0.16336. Upstream's master steps each coupon to its payment date and discounts each step's premium from the step's end, as the fork did. |
| [#275](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/275) (2026-11-18) | `ql/experimental/credit/{basket,integralcdoengine,integralntdengine,midpointcdoengine}.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/{cdo,nthtodefault}.cpp` | Default curves with settlement days start a day or two after today and don't reach back before their reference dates, so a loss model that reads them throws "negative time" when it's asked for a statistic at an earlier date. `MidPointCDOEngine` took each coupon's expected tranche loss on its accrual end and `IntegralCDOEngine` on each integration step's end, so each threw where that date fell within the curves' lag after today. `IntegralNtdEngine`, on a discount curve as of today, asked for the probability of the nth default on today, and threw on every price on a model that reads the curves. On #275's table, with every default curve two days after today and a discount curve as of today, the engines threw on 540 CDO rows and 31 nth-to-default rows. Each engine now takes every expected tranche loss and every probability on the later of its date and the latest reference date of the default curves of the names left, the rule the fix for [#228](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/228) set for the CDO engines' first expected loss; one helper, `detail::latestDefaultCurveDate`, gives the three engines that date. Where those curves all start on one date, that's the value on curves from today with no hazard until then and the same hazards after it, and none of #275's rows throws. Where they start on different dates, no protection counts before the latest of them ([#331](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/331)). `IntegralNtdEngine` keeps its steps, and a step that ends before that date counts no default. `RandomDefaultLM` and `RandomLossLM` count each name from its own curve's date, so they priced where the others threw; they now move where the curves start on different dates: over eight names, four on curves from today and four from 30 days later, a first to default's protection by 1.2e-2, relatively, and on 2006-11-30 the CDO engines' by 6.5e-5 (mid-point) and 1.3e-4 (integral). |
| [#329](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/329) (2026-11-19) | `ql/experimental/credit/{integralcdoengine,midpointcdoengine}.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | `IntegralCDOEngine` and `MidPointCDOEngine` counted protection only over the coupons not yet paid. A coupon can be paid before its accrual end, on an Unadjusted schedule under Preceding payments or under ModifiedFollowing when the end is a weekend at a month's end, so neither engine counted the protection over the days between, and both started it from the expected tranche loss on the next coupon's accrual start. On #329's table (eight names, the Gaussian LHP model at 0.3, the 0-10 % tranche, the coupon to Sunday 2008-06-01 paid on Friday 05-30) the integral engine's protection leg fell 0.089 on the payment day and rose 0.019 on each of the next two. Both engines now keep a coupon that has been paid but still accrues after today: it counts its protection to its accrual end, and no premium, and reports its expected tranche loss as an unpaid coupon does, where it reported 0. The protection starts from the expected tranche loss on the latest of today, the default curves' date and the accrual start of the first coupon not yet paid or still accruing, so a forward-starting contract still counts none before its first accrual start. On #329's table the leg now falls 0.0174 a day in the integral engine, and the mid-point engine's 0.0173 from 05-30 to 05-31. Under Following payments nothing moves, and on #275's grid 16 of 7,488 rows move, all under Preceding payments on the day a coupon accruing to a Saturday is paid. Upstream's master skips a paid coupon too. |

Under Following and Unadjusted payments the last payment is on or after the maturity, and nothing moves. Upstream's master has the same code. |
| [#333](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/333) (2026-11-19) | `ql/experimental/credit/midpointcdoengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | On a discount curve dated after today, as one built with settlement days, `MidPointCDOEngine` ran each coupon's period from the latest of its accrual start, today and the curve's date, and ended it no earlier. On #333's case (the 0-10 % tranche on the Gaussian LHP model, a schedule to Saturday 2007-09-01, valued on Thursday 08-30 on a curve dated Monday 09-03) its protection was 0.144617, against `IntegralCDOEngine`'s 0.072320. On #275's grid its other valuations on such curves move by at most 2.6e-5 of the protection. Upstream's master starts each period on the later of the coupon's accrual start and the curve's date. |
| [#328](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/328) (2026-11-19) | `ql/experimental/credit/{integralcdoengine,midpointcdoengine}.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/cdo.cpp` | On a discount curve dated after today, both CDO engines discounted a premium paid after today but before the curve's date at a date the curve doesn't reach, and threw "negative time": on 50 of the 2,080 rows of #328's sweep, on the day or two before a payment. | [#294](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/294) (2026-11-19) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::simpson(absTolerance, maxEvaluations = 1000)` and `trapezoid` passed their second argument to `SimpsonIntegral` and `TrapezoidIntegral<Default>`, whose constructors read it as a bound on the rules' estimates. Each estimate halves the intervals of the one before, so the n-th rests on 2^(n-1) + 1 evaluations, and the default of 1000 bounded nothing: where two estimates in a row never agreed to the tolerance, the call didn't return. A probe for [#239](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/239) stalled that way, five runs of five, on Simpson with the Andersen-Piterbarg control variate at the double next to rho = -1. The bound keeps counting estimates, which every existing call relies on, is renamed `maxIterations` to say so, and defaults to 24, so a rule throws "max number of iterations reached" after 8,388,609 evaluations an integral; the header says what the count means and where a call needs a larger one. A call that converged within 24 estimates returns what it returned, on the same evaluations. Near rho = -1 and 1 (rho ±0.95 to ±0.999, 420 rows) Gatheral's formula now throws on 92 rows with Simpson and 93 with the trapezoid rule, all at ±0.99 and ±0.999: the parent priced 25 and 30 of them on the 25th estimate, and the other 67 and 63 needed more than 25, or never converge. At 1e-12 the trapezoid rule with Gatheral's formula throws on 35 rows of a grid with rho from -0.9 to 0.9 that the parent priced on the 25th estimate. Counting evaluations instead, as the old name said, would have broken two of the test suite's five calls. Upstream's factories, moved to `FourierIntegration`, still pass the bound the same way. |
| [#299](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/299) (2026-11-19) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::andersenPiterbargIntegrationLimit(c_inf, epsilon, v0, t)` returns the larger of two roots. The first, of c_inf u + log(u) = L with L = -log(epsilon), came from Brent's search stepping down from the guess L / c_inf, whose fourth step lands at -0.76 L / c_inf, where the logarithm is NaN; so wherever log(u) at the root was more than 68 % of L, the search threw "unable to bracket root in 100 function evaluations". At epsilon 1e-17 that is from c_inf 4e-11 down, as the issue measured, and a loosened `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` gets there at ordinary c_inf: at 1e-4, with rho -0.5, v0 = theta = 1e-4, kappa 1, sigma 0.5 and two years at the money, every rule that takes the limit threw. The search now runs between L / (1 + c_inf) and the smaller of (L + max(0, log(c_inf / L))) / c_inf and 1 / epsilon, bounds on either side of the root, and returns that top where rounding leaves the left side at most L there; the function also refuses a c_inf that isn't finite, and a top past the largest double. On a grid of 12,640 calls the parent threw on 1,861 and the fix throws on none, and where the parent returned the two agree to 6.9e-16. Through the engine with the AndersenPiterbarg formula at `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` 1e-4 and 1e-8 (1,024 prices) the parent threw on 816: 407 now price, 360 of them within max(1e-6, andersenPiterbargEpsilon) of references that take no limit, and the other 409 throw from the rule, at its bound or its count of evaluations; every price the parent gave, the fix gives to 1.1e-14. The limit's Gaussian part, taken at v0 where the control variates decay at the average variance, is [#339](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/339), and the constant e^{Re psi} that c_inf's root leaves out is [#340](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/340). |
| [#339](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/339) (2026-11-19) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::andersenPiterbargIntegrationLimit(c_inf, epsilon, v, t)` takes the larger of two roots, where e^{-c_inf u} / u falls to epsilon and where the Gaussian e^{-v t u^2 / 2} / u does, and `calculate()` and the deprecated `doCalculation` gave it v0 as v. Below u of about 1 / (sigma t) the characteristic function decays as a Gaussian at the average variance vAvg = theta + (v0 - theta) (1 - e^{-kappa t}) / (kappa t), as the AndersenPiterbarg and AngledContour control variates do. So where v0 was far below theta the limit lay orders of magnitude past where the integrand had decayed, 311,142 where vAvg's is 785 in the issue's case, and Gauss-Kronrod at 1e-8 stopped after its first 15 evaluations, 0.85 to 97.9 off by form; where v0 was far above theta it fell short, 0.019 to 0.545 off. Both now pass vAvg, and the parameter is named v. Against references that take no limit, on a sweep of kappa 0.1, 5 and 10 with every form (26,973 prices) the prices off by more than ten times their tolerance, or throwing, fall from 10,275 to 3,025, with 7,380 gained and 130 lost, and below theta at the default epsilons (2,268 prices) from 454 to 14; at v0 = theta every price is the parent's to the bit. Of the 130 lost, 111 are where c_inf's root leaves out the constant e^{Re psi} that the limit at v0 had covered ([#340](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/340)), 18 need more than Gauss-Lobatto's default budget of 1,000 evaluations, and 1 is Gauss-Lobatto stopping where both its estimates are off ([#341](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/341)). Simpson's rule and the trapezoid rule, whose nodes are fixed, spread them over the longer limit above theta: at their default of 1,000 nodes 3,071 prices are gained there and 23 lost, the worst 1.9e-4 off, and each one lost is within 8.8e-7 by 16,000 nodes. `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine` keeps v0 ([#344](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/344)), and at rho = -1 and 1 `hestonUnitCorrelationLimit` is as it was. |

Since upstream's 2023-07 update to the engine the helper's integrand carries (F / K)^alpha, so away from the forward `doCalculation`'s correction was sqrt(K / F) times `calculate()`'s: on 16 rows struck at 80 and 125 it came back 0.023 to 0.188 off with AndersenPiterbarg and OptimalCV, and up to 3,539 off with AsymptoticChF, whose control variate carries e^{Re psi}. It now takes F / pi, and on the 16 rows prices within 7.3e-14 of exp-sinh (4.9e-12 with AsymptoticChF), as `calculate()` does. Removing `doCalculation`, the issue's other option, changes the public interface and is the owner's call; nothing in `ql/` or `Examples/` calls it. `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine`'s sqrt(K F) / pi stays: its helper carries no (F / K)^alpha. |

The exponential leaves out the asymptotic's constant e^{Re psi}, so where Re psi is positive its root stopped short (#340); between the two regimes phi follows neither, so the larger root could stop short there too (#347). `calculate()` and the deprecated `doCalculation` now keep that root, u0, only where the ratio |phi| / u there is at most epsilon or at most `QL_EPSILON`. Elsewhere they double past u0 until the ratio, read off the real part of `lnChF`, is below epsilon, and solve between the last two points by Brent; where a read isn't finite, or nothing brackets by 2^64 u0, the limit stays u0. At the default `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` of 1e-25, and the 1e-40 the constructor taking a tolerance and a budget passes, the read never opened on anything measured: 89,952 and 24,426 prices are the parent's to the bit. On the issues' rows, by Gauss-Kronrod at 1e-8 against exp-sinh, the forms came back up to 34.9 times epsilon off, and now within 0.033 times. With Gauss-Kronrod at 1e-12, on the 1,839 parameter sets of a 127,008-row sweep where the ratio at the parent's limit was over e times epsilon, AndersenPiterbarg was more than epsilon off on 114, up to 5.70 times, and AngledContourNoCV on 49, up to 4.39; now on none, at most 0.09 and 0.08 times. On twelve sweeps of the production rules at loosened epsilons, the prices of the forms other than AsymptoticChF off by more than the larger of 5e-7 and epsilon, or throwing, fall from 5,435 to 1,854 of 59,286, with 3,634 gained and 53 lost: 49 are Gauss-Lobatto's relative tolerance running out of its budget ([#297](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/297)), and 4 Gauss-Kronrod accepting an interval whose two estimates are both off ([#346](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/346)). AsymptoticChF gains 327 and loses 1, at Re psi 15 ([#342](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/342)). At an absolute 1e-8 alone, Gauss-Lobatto gains 1,003 of five sweeps' 21,459 prices and loses 14, 13 of them stopping early on the longer range ([#341](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/341)) and 1 at its budget. Of the 111 prices [#339](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/339) lost to the missing constant, 110 now price within their tolerance and 1 runs out of Gauss-Lobatto's budget. On [#299](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/299)'s grid of 1,024 prices at 1e-4 and 1e-8, 116 move, by at most 0.69 times their tolerance, and none crosses it, so its figures stand. The read costs about 1,300 instructions a limit at the default epsilon and about 11,000 at 1e-8 and 1e-6, against the closed form's 2,800. `testSimpsonAndTrapezoidStopAtTheirBound` (#294) now pins Simpson's rule at its 8th estimate, where the longer limit takes it. `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine` takes the same read with [#344](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/344)'s fix, and at rho = -1 and 1 `hestonUnitCorrelationLimit` is as it was. |

It now asks once, and every price measured is the one before, to the bit. On a grid of 729 calls with AndersenPiterbarg and OptimalCV a price costs about 4,000 instructions less at the default `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` (1.0 to 1.1 % by Gauss-Kronrod, 0.4 % by Gauss-Lobatto) and 13,800 to 14,100 less at 1e-8 and 1e-6; with #340, 0.4 % and 0.2 % less at the default than before either, and 5.6 to 7.1 % and 3.9 to 6.6 % more at 1e-8 and 1e-6, where #340's limit takes more of the integrand. |

| [#348](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/348), [#355](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/355) (2026-11-19) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/experimental/forward/analytichestonforwardeuropeanengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp`, `test-suite/forwardoption.cpp` | Where sigma was at most 1e-6 and kappa at least 1e-8, `AnalyticHestonEngine::chF` took its value from an expansion to second order in sigma, whose coefficients are sums of terms of the order of theta that cancel as kappa t falls, divided by 2 kappa, 2 kappa^2 and 16 kappa^4, so where kappa t was small their rounding swamped what was left (#348). At sigma 1e-6, kappa 1e-7, v0 0.01 and rho -0.9, the default engine's one-month call came out 1.20650 against 1.22575 at a strike of 100, and -0.00177 against 7.0e-11 at 120. Every form but Gatheral's and BranchCorrection takes `chF`, the default constructor's OptimalCV among them, and so does `ExponentialFittingHestonEngine`. `chF` is now exp(`lnChF`) on every branch: `lnChF`, which it took above the switch, writes the same function in Andersen and Lake's form, which doesn't cancel there. The two readers that followed the same switch, `AP_Helper`'s unit-correlation path at rho = -1 and 1 and `AnalyticHestonForwardEuropeanEngine`'s `forwardHestonLogChF`, read `lnChF` too. `lnChF`'s general form multiplies kappa theta / sigma^2 by a factor of order sigma^2, which is NaN where sigma^2 is 0: at sigma = 0, which `setParams` can set though `HestonModel`'s constructor refuses it, and below about 2.2e-162. Where kappa was below 1e-8 those sigmas reached it already, and the default engine priced at NaN there (#355). `lnChF` now returns sigma = 0's limit wherever sigma is at most 1e-100, the variance following its mean. Against references in 40 digits: on #348's grid at sigma 1e-7 and 1e-6 (864 rows) the default constructor's prices, and OptimalCV's and AngledContour's on exp-sinh, were up to 0.019 off and are now within 1.5e-13; at short tenors and small v0, the prices of the six forms that take `chF` on Gauss-Lobatto within their tolerance went from 56 to 3,348 of 3,888 at kappa 1e-6, with none lost; at rho = -1 and 1, 230 prices that threw on Gauss-Lobatto now price, and none is lost; and at sigma 0 to 1e-50, set through `setParams`, no price of eleven engines is NaN, against 696 of 3,240, and every price is within 7.5e-14 of Black's at the integrated variance but those of the two forms without a control variate on Gauss-Lobatto, as before. The forward engine's reset today was up to 5.5e-2 off and is now within 2.8e-14; a reset at half the expiry moved by up to 8.1e-2 and is within 1.5e-9 of an expansion whose own error is of order sigma^4, and what's left is the mass the engine's density for the variance at the reset misses ([#356](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/356)). Above sigma 1e-6 nothing moves: #340's sweeps, 258,759 lines, are the same to the bit. On the parent as here, at rho = -1 and 1 the unit-correlation limit fails more as sigma falls ([#354](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/354)), Gauss-Lobatto's relative tolerance runs out on what the control variate leaves ([#297](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/297)), and past 0.15 years the default constructor and OptimalCV take AsymptoticChF, whose control variate throws or is NaN at these sigmas ([#300](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/300)). The Gatheral and BranchCorrection forms and `COSHestonEngine` write the characteristic function their own way ([#349](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/349), [#350](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/350)). |
| [#349](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/349), [#350](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/350), [#361](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/361) (2026-11-20) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/coshestonengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp`, `test-suite/batesmodel.cpp` | `AnalyticHestonEngine`'s Gatheral and BranchCorrection forms and `COSHestonEngine::chF` wrote the Heston characteristic function with t1 - d, COS's g - D, as a difference. Where sigma phi is small against kappa the two nearly cancel, and the formula carried their rounding into the exponent over sigma^2 (#350): at sigma 1e-7 and kappa 1, `COSHestonEngine` priced a ten-year call at 100 at 29.533 against 29.608. Where sigma was at most 1e-5 the Gatheral form took an expansion that holds only where sigma phi is small against kappa, and elsewhere its integrand could grow instead of decaying (#349): at sigma 1e-5 and kappa 1e-7, `BatesEngine` by default priced a one-month call at 80 at 6.2e106 against 20.11, and the same call at a year at NaN. The three now take the function from `lnChF`'s form, Andersen and Lake's, whose body moves unchanged into `detail::hestonLnChF`, which all three call: nothing rounded is divided by sigma^2, and at sigma <= 1e-100 they take sigma = 0's limit (#355). The BranchCorrection form is now Gatheral's, to the bit: its logarithm no longer crosses the cut, so the branch counter goes; and it takes the Gatheral form's limit at phi = 0, where it had none and returned NaN on the discrete Simpson and trapezoid rules (#361). The enum value and the constructor's refusal of BranchCorrection with an adaptive rule stay. Against references in 40 digits on 1,296 rows at sigma 1e-7 to 1e-5, the Gatheral form on Gauss-Lobatto at 1e-12 was up to 28.4 off and is within 7.6e-12, the BranchCorrection form on 192 nodes was up to 0.199 off and is within 2.0e-13, and `COSHestonEngine` was up to 0.0748 off and is within 8.2e-14; at sigma 0 to 1e-50, against Black's formula at the integrated variance, none of the three is NaN, where the BranchCorrection form was on 1,260 of 1,620 rows, and all are within 2.5e-13. At ordinary parameters prices move by at most 1.26e-11, and a price takes 38 % fewer instructions under the Gatheral form on Gauss-Laguerre and 45 % fewer on Gauss-Lobatto. The 72 rows of the grid that still throw on Gauss-Lobatto are those where its relative tolerance can't be met ([#297](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/297)). `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine` writes t1 - d the same way ([#360](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/360)), `COSHestonEngine`'s truncation range takes cumulants that cancel at small kappa ([#359](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/359)), and `Fj_Helper`'s limits at phi = 0 difference exponentials ([#363](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/363)). |

That limit came from the Heston part alone and left out the add-on term's part, the slope of its imaginary part at 0, so every engine with an add-on term took a wrong value at that node: `BatesEngine`, `BatesDetJumpEngine`, `BatesDoubleExpEngine`, `BatesDoubleExpDetJumpEngine`, `AnalyticHestonHullWhiteEngine` and `AnalyticH1HWEngine` on Gauss-Lobatto, through their constructors that take a relative tolerance, and a user's subclass on any rule that takes phi = 0 as a node (Gauss-Lobatto, Simpson's and the trapezoid rule, and the discrete Simpson and trapezoid rules). Against references that never evaluate phi = 0, on Gauss-Lobatto at relative 1e-8 19 prices of 1,933, 0.14 to 1.38 off at kappa 1e-8 to 1e-6 and v0 = theta, are within 5.1e-10, and no price is now beyond the tolerance's scale; over each set of rows evaluations fall 24 to 37 % at 1e-8, and 11 to 21 % at 1e-12. At 1e-12, four prices at kappa 1e-9 go from about 2e-12 off to 1.4e-10 and 1.5e-10, 1.9 and 2.0 times the scale, where the Heston part's own limit is about 2e-9 off ([#363](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/363)). Engines with no add-on term price as before, to the bit. |
| [#365](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/365) (2026-11-20) | `ql/math/integrals/gausslobattointegral.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/integrals.cpp` | `GaussLobattoIntegral::calculateAbsTolerance` returned the smaller of the absolute tolerance and acc relTol, acc being the 13-point Kronrod estimate of the integral over the whole range. Where acc was negative, so was acc relTol, and the min took it whatever the absolute tolerance; the step, which reads a magnitude, then stopped a panel once its two estimates agreed within \|acc\| relTol. So, given both tolerances, an integral whose estimate was negative was taken to \|acc\| relTol, where a positive one was taken to the smaller of the two. With both at 1e-8 the integral of -e^x over [0, 10] came back 3.0e-8 off after 110 evaluations, where that of e^x came back exact after 260, and `AnalyticHestonEngine` with AsymptoticChF on `Integration::gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8)` priced [#341](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/341)'s 3-day call 2.5e-6 off its reference, its estimate being -1,312.25 against an integral of -266.21. The tolerance is now \|acc\| relTol. Every step is symmetric under negation, so the integral of -f is now minus that of f, bit for bit, after as many evaluations, and the 3-day call comes back 4.0e-12 off, after 920 evaluations instead of 320. With an absolute tolerance alone nothing moves; with a relative one alone the absolute one is `Null<Real>()`, 3.4e38, and only an integral whose \|acc\| relTol exceeds that moves: it throws, as it already did for a positive estimate. So in the library only `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::gaussLobatto` given both tolerances reaches the change; the engines' constructors pass no absolute tolerance. On #341's sweep of 1,296 parameter sets and the engine's seven forms on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8, 10000)`, against exp-sinh at 1e-12, in units of 1e-8 (S e^{-qT} + K e^{-rT}) / 2, about the price error the tolerance allows, 25 prices come from 1.001 to 3,490 units to within 0.64 of one, and 138 AsymptoticChF prices that came back 7.7e5 to 1.5e288 units off throw instead; no price leaves the unit, none within it throws, and the prices take 3.6 % more evaluations. At `Integration::gaussLobatto`'s default budget of 1,000 the change makes prices that came in throw. On [#342](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/342)'s grids of 729 and 1,728 calls on a spot of 100, each priced at an `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` of 1e-25, 1e-8 and 1e-6, on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8)`, 174 AsymptoticChF prices at Re psi 6.1 to 17 that came within 1e-6 throw, and so do 359 that were further off, while 4 that were off come within it: there the integrand is about e^{Re psi} times the price's scale, and the absolute tolerance, now held, takes more than the budget. The commit's message has 172 and 361, counted against 5e-7 at an `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` of 1e-8, where two prices 7.2e-7 and 9.8e-7 off throw now ([#342](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/342#issuecomment-7048262531)). No AngledContour price moves there. The min against an absolute tolerance is QuantLib's own, not that of Gander and Gautschi's adaptlob, which the class follows, and upstream's file has the same line. |
| [#341](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/341) (2026-11-20) | `ql/math/integrals/gausslobattointegral.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analyticptdhestonengine.hpp`, `test-suite/integrals.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `GaussLobattoIntegral` accepts a panel once its 4-point Gauss-Lobatto and 7-point Kronrod estimates agree to the tolerance, and the two can agree on a panel whose nodes miss the integrand's shape. On `Integration::gaussLobatto(1e-8, Null<Real>(), 10000)`, `AnalyticHestonEngine`'s AngledContourNoCV price of a ten-year call at the money came back 6.9e-4 off: the first of the six panels the range splits into carries most of the integral, and it passed with its two estimates 6.5e-9 apart, inside the 7.5e-9 the tolerance allows there, while both were 2.4e-5 off. The Gatheral form's price of a ten-year call came back 5.0e-6 off the same way. `GaussLobattoIntegral` now takes a minimum depth, 0 by default: a panel fewer than that many splits below the whole range is split whatever its estimates. `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::gaussLobatto` takes it as a new last argument, 2 by default, so the engines' (relTolerance, maxEvaluations) constructors, which go through it, take 2: `AnalyticHestonEngine`'s, `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine`'s, `BatesEngine`'s and `BatesDoubleExpEngine`'s with their DetJump subclasses', `AnalyticHestonHullWhiteEngine`'s and, through it, `AnalyticH1HWEngine`'s. The two calls come back 1.0e-10 and 2.9e-10 off. Only `Integration::gaussLobatto` sets the depth: under a global depth of 2, `testNoArbSabrInterpolation`'s volatilities came out 4.2e-12 to 4.0e-9 off the values it expects, against its tolerance of 1e-12, and at depth 0 every other caller's values are as they were, bit for bit. On #341's sweep of 1,296 parameter sets and the engine's seven forms against exp-sinh at 1e-12, in units of 1e-8 (S e^{-qT} + K e^{-rT}) / 2, 62 prices come within one unit on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, Null<Real>(), 10000)`, the constructors' rule, 8 AngledContourNoCV from 1.0 to 839 units, the Gatheral row from 6.13 and 53 AsymptoticChF from 1.1 to 3,489, and 3 on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8, 10000)`; no price leaves the unit, and outside AsymptoticChF none is left outside it on either rule. On [#363](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/363)'s grid of 1,080 calls at small sigma and kappa, the Gatheral form's worst price on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8, 10000)` goes from 1.05 off to 5.7e-8. It costs evaluations: an integral takes at least 230 at depth 2, against 20 at 0, and throws unless the budget is above 225, and the sweep's prices take 19.0 % more. At `Integration::gaussLobatto`'s default budget of 1,000, 100 of the sweep's prices that took 890 to 980 evaluations, or 1,870 to 1,930 for the Gatheral form's two integrals, now throw, and on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8)` so do 81 AsymptoticChF and 14 AngledContour prices on [#342](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/342)'s grids that came within 1e-6. On [#296](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/296)'s three models, with calls struck at 0.6 to 1.5 times the forward, `AnalyticHestonEngine(model, 1e-8, 1000)` throws on the 41 of the 12,070 prices it gave before that took 890 to 980 evaluations, which came within a relative 4.0e-14; at a budget of 10,000 none does ([#374](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/374)). On the 124 rows of #296's table that have a reference, AndersenPiterbargOptCV on `gaussLobatto(1e-10, Null<Real>(), 1000000)` came 1.1e-2 to 30 times the price off on 34 calls from 1 - \|rho\| = 1e-6 on, and throws on them now; the simple constructor throws on none of the rows. `GaussLobattoIntegral` gains a member, and its constructor and protected step a parameter each, so code compiled against the old header must be rebuilt. Three rows of [#239](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/239)'s tests that passed at depth 2 with what they guard taken out are also priced at a minimum depth of 0, and the simple constructor's comment now says it takes OptimalCV, as it has since `918676984` (2023-11-26), where it said Gatheral's form. What stays outside the unit is AsymptoticChF's: 329 prices on the constructors' rule, 1.02 to 1.1e41 units off (#342). |
| [#363](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/363) (2026-11-20) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | At phi = 0 `AnalyticHestonEngine::Fj_Helper` takes the P_j integrand as its limit: log(F / K) and an add-on term's slope ([#362](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/362)), plus half the variance integrated under P1's measure for P1, and less half of it under P2's for P2. It took that half variance as differences of exponentials in x = kmr t for P1, kmr being rho sigma - kappa, and x = -kappa t for P2, so their rounding was divided by kmr^2 or kappa^2; and where \|kmr\| <= 1e-7 P1 took the first terms, which drop those of order x. Against 50 digits, at sigma 0.1, rho 0.5 and theta 0.04, P1's was 2.1e-5 off a limit of 0.0016 with kmr 1.1e-7 on either side of 0 and a month, and P2's 2.2e-9 off at kappa 1e-9 and ten years. The discrete Simpson and trapezoid rules take phi = 0 as a node, and Gauss-Lobatto, Simpson's and the trapezoid rule as an endpoint, under the Gatheral and BranchCorrection forms, for `AnalyticHestonEngine` and the engines that derive from it. Both now take it as (kappa theta t^2 f2(x) + v0 t f1(x)) / 2, with f1(x) = (e^x - 1) / x and f2(x) = (e^x - 1 - x) / x^2, the old closed form term by term: where \|x\| < 0.5, f2 is its series nested to x / 16 and f1 = 1 + x f2, and elsewhere both come from expm1. Against 50 digits on the issue's rows and a sweep of \|x\| from 1e-12 to 50, of both signs, for P1 and P2, at t from a day to 30 years (3,880 rows), the half variance comes within a relative 4.4e-16 on every row, where the old forms were up to 3.5 times it off on P1 and 7.4e-4 of it on P2. The change needs [#341](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/341)'s minimum depth under it: without it, on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8, 10000)`, the corrected limit lets Gauss-Lobatto stop on panels whose two estimates both miss, and at sigma 0.001 and 0.01, v0 = theta, ten years and a strike of 100, 20 of the 24 prices come back 3.9e-4 to 1.05 off; with every panel split twice first, all 24 come within 3.7e-12. On #363's grid of 1,080 calls at small sigma and kappa and near kappa = rho sigma, the Gatheral form against Lewis's integral in 40 digits, prices more than 1e-12 off on `gaussLobatto(1e-12, 1e-12, 200000)` go from 323 to 8, and the worst from 7.1e-11 to 1.7e-11, at 1.8 % fewer evaluations; on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, 1e-8, 10000)` prices more than 1e-9 off go from 245 to 4, and the worst from 5.7e-8 to 1.8e-9; and on `gaussLobatto(1e-8, Null<Real>(), 10000)`, the rule the engines' (relTolerance, maxEvaluations) constructors take, from 246 to 4, at 1.2 % fewer evaluations. Gauss-Laguerre's prices don't move, and on no rule does a price throw that didn't, or stop throwing. On #362's grid of 2,142 calls under the Bates and Hull-White engines, at 1e-12, the four prices that #362's fix left 1.9 to 2.0 times the tolerance's price scale off land on the reference, and no price leaves the scale; on #341's sweep no price moves at the 15 digits it prints. `COSHestonEngine`'s cumulants are [#359](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/359). |
| [#367](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/367) (2026-11-20) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/batesengine.cpp`, `test-suite/batesmodel.cpp` | `BatesDetJumpEngine` and `BatesDoubleExpDetJumpEngine` scale their constant-intensity engine's add-on term, which is linear in the intensity, by the intensity's average over the option's life in units of lambda: the intensity runs from lambda to thetaLambda at the rate kappaLambda, so the average is thetaLambda + (lambda - thetaLambda) D(y), with D(y) = (1 - e^{-y}) / y and y = kappaLambda t. They took the ratio as thetaLambda / lambda (y - 1 + e^{-y}) / y + (1 - e^{-y}) / y, two differences of exponentials divided by y, at every node of the integral, so a factor of about 1 came out about 1e-16 / y off. At thetaLambda = lambda, where the intensity stays at lambda and each engine must give its constant-intensity engine's price, calls at strikes of 80 to 120 came back up to 1.4e-4 off at kappaLambda 1e-12 and lambda 1 on a month, and 2e-8 off at kappaLambda 1e-8. Both now take it as thetaLambda / lambda + (1 - thetaLambda / lambda) D(y), the same expression rearranged, with D from `detail::decayRatio`, -expm1(-y) / y, so that nothing is divided by y. Against 50 digits on 6,000 rows, y from 1e-16 to 50 and thetaLambda / lambda from 1e-4 to 10, the factor comes within 1.9e-15, the worst at thetaLambda / lambda 10, where the rearranged form cancels a digit; the old form was up to 0.11 off, at y 1e-16. At thetaLambda = lambda the factor is exactly 1, so each engine gives its constant-intensity engine's price bit for bit, and from kappaLambda 0.01 up, calls at a month, a year and ten years, lambda 0.1 to 1, move by at most 2.2e-14. |
| [#369](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/369) (2026-11-20) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytich1hwengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hybridhestonhullwhiteprocess.cpp` | `AnalyticH1HWEngine::Fj_Helper` returns eta rhoSr I4, the term the stock's correlation with the Hull-White rate adds to the log characteristic function, eta being the Hull-White sigma. I4's bracket is the engine's fit of E[sqrt(v(t))], a + b e^{-ct}, integrated against 1 - e^{-lambda (T - t)} over [0, T], lambda being the Hull-White a, so it is of order lambda T^2 as lambda goes to 0, and finite at c = lambda; but it was taken as differences of terms of order T, divided by lambda, one of its a-terms by lambda again, and its last term by c - lambda. At kappa 1.5, theta 0.04, sigma 0.3 and v0 0.04, where the fit's rate c is 2.9496, calls at 100 were NaN on a month, a year and ten years at a Hull-White a equal to c, and within three ulps of it a month's call was up to 9.1e-4 off; at a Hull-White a of 1e-8 a month's call was 0.19 off, and at 1e-10 it was NaN. I4 is now -(u^2 -+ iu) T^2 [a phi(lambda T) + b R(cT, (lambda - c) T)], with phi(x) = (e^{-x} - 1 + x) / x^2, R(u, v) = (D(u) - D(u + v)) / v and D(x) = (1 - e^{-x}) / x, from `ql/math/expm1.hpp`'s `detail::decayPhiAndH` and `detail::decayRatioDifference`: the same bracket with lambda cancelled, which divides by nothing that can vanish. Against the old bracket in 113-bit arithmetic, the engine being the same in every other respect: on the series branch at small kappa, on `testH1HWPricingEngine`'s ten calls and next to where c equals the Hull-White a, 88 finite prices are within 1.4e-14, and NaN on the same 72 rows as the reference, where the fit is NaN ([#368](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/368)); at Hull-White a from 0.03 down to 1e-10, 54 prices are within 4e-15; with v0 around the fit's a^2, 31 finite prices are within 2.1e-14, and NaN on the same 47 ([#371](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/371)); and at Hull-White a 0.1 to 3, 180 are within 1.4e-14. 113 bits aren't enough at a Hull-White a of 1e-12, and a 50-digit evaluation of the bracket, for the closed-form fit, puts the new form within a relative 3.2e-16 of it at 1e-8, 1e-10 and 1e-12, a month to ten years. `testH1HWPricingEngine`'s implied volatilities move by at most 5.6e-16, against its tolerance of 1e-4, and on [#362](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/362)'s grid 28 of the 36 `AnalyticH1HWEngine` calls move, by at most 5.8e-11. On `testH1HWPricingEngine`'s calls the instructions rise 1.3 % at sigma 0.3, where the fit takes its closed form, and 1.1 % at 0.6, where it sums its series. The fit is recomputed at every node ([#370](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/370)). |
| [#368](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/368) (2026-11-20) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytich1hwengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hybridhestonhullwhiteprocess.cpp` | Where 8 kappa theta / sigma^2 is at most 1, `AnalyticH1HWEngine` fits E[sqrt(v(t))] as a + b e^{-ct} through its value at 0, sqrt(v0), and at 1/kappa, `Fj_Helper::Lambda(1/kappa)`, which sums the noncentral chi-square's series in d = 4 kappa theta / sigma^2 and λ = λ(t). The series stopped once a term fell below float epsilon, 1.2e-7, whatever the sum. Its first term is about 0.886 d for small d, so where d is below about 1.35e-7 it stopped after that term, Lambda(1/kappa) came out below a, and c was the logarithm of a negative number: at v0 = theta 0.04 and sigma 0.3 every price was NaN from kappa 7.5e-8 down, and at 7.6e-8 c was a relative 2.3e-8 off. Elsewhere it dropped terms that are small against 1 but not against the sum, and where λ(1/kappa) is above about 725 it refused ("can not calculate Lambda"), as the terms took more than 1000 steps to fall below float epsilon. The series now stops once a term is below `QL_EPSILON` times the sum: its terms rise to their largest and then fall, so no term that small comes before the largest. Where λ is above about 1413 the sum overflows, and the engine refuses a sum that isn't finite, with the old message, rather than return NaN ([#372](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/372)). Against the previous commit's reference, which takes I4 from the bracket [#369](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/369) replaced in 113-bit arithmetic, here with this stop: on the series branch at small kappa, on `testH1HWPricingEngine`'s ten calls and next to where c equals the Hull-White a, the 72 prices that were NaN, at kappa 7.5e-8 and below, are finite, and all 160 are within 1.42e-14, while where the previous commit priced, prices move by at most 9.6e-10; at Hull-White a from 0.03 down to 1e-10, 54 prices are within 7.11e-15; with v0 around the fit's a^2, 31 finite prices are within 7.11e-15, and NaN on the same 47 as before ([#371](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/371)); and at Hull-White a 0.1 to 3, 180 are within 1.07e-14. At large v0, on 864 prices, every price the previous commit gave is still given, 54 it refused, where λ(1/kappa) is 864 to 873, are priced, 189 are still refused, where it is 1455 and above, and the 621 that both give move by at most 4.9e-9; on two models a month's call at 100 is refused from λ(1/kappa) 1414, where the previous commit, like master, refused it from 725 and 726. On the five models where the moves are largest the fit's c is within 1.8e-15 of a 40-digit evaluation, where the old stop's was 8.3e-10 to 2.4e-8 off, so the moves are the old stop's error. `testH1HWPricingEngine`'s implied volatilities move by at most 8.09e-12, against its tolerance of 1e-4, and [#362](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/362)'s grid is byte for byte the same. On `testH1HWPricingEngine`'s calls the instructions rise 11.0 % at sigma 0.6, where the fit sums its series, as the series takes more terms, and are unchanged at 0.3, where it takes its closed form. Where v0 is a little above the fit's a^2 the fit is still NaN (#371), and the fit is computed at every integration node ([#370](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/370)). |
| [#342](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/342) (2026-11-21) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `AnalyticHestonEngine`'s AsymptoticChF integrates the characteristic function less its asymptotic at large u, and adds back the asymptotic's closed form, which carries e^{psi}, with Re psi = [(kappa - rho sigma / 2)(v0 + kappa theta t) + kappa theta log(4 (1 - rho^2))] / sigma^2. Near u = 0 the control variate's modulus is e^{Re psi}, so where Re psi is large the price is what is left of two terms each about e^{Re psi} times its size, and a rule's error on the integral reaches the price multiplied by e^{Re psi}. `optimalControlVariate` takes AsymptoticChF only where Re psi is below 0.1, but an explicit AsymptoticChF was taken at any Re psi by `priceVanillaPayoff` (so `calculate()` and `HestonBlackVolSurface`), the deprecated `doCalculation`, and `ExponentialFittingHestonEngine` with cv = AsymptoticChF, and came out off with nothing to say so. On calls on a spot of 100 (r 3 %, q 1 %, theta 0.04, strikes 80 to 125), on a grid of ordinary parameters and on #342's own, against references that agree within 1e-7, Gauss-Laguerre at 128 nodes was off by up to 2.1e-6 at Re psi 0.1 to 1, 0.012 at 7 to 10 and 5.6e286 beyond 300, and `ExponentialFittingHestonEngine` by up to 4.6e-4 at 0.1 to 1, 4.4 at 7 to 10 and 4.3e16 at 10 to 50; exp-sinh at 1e-8 came out off from Re psi 2.4 and Gauss-Kronrod at 1e-8 from 10.6, and Gauss-Lobatto ran out of evaluations from 1.1. `AP_Helper`'s constructor, which the three paths share, now refuses an explicit AsymptoticChF where Re psi is 0.1 or more, or NaN, beside [#239](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/239)'s refusal at rho = -1 and 1, and its message says that OptimalCV takes AngledContour there. OptimalCV never meets the refusal: the two compare one Re psi, computed out of line (`asymptoticChFRePsi`), as inlined copies that GCC contracts into fused multiply-adds differently could fall on either side of the bound within an ulp of it. psi itself is computed as before, so below the bound every price keeps its bits: of 134,807 probe rows, both grids on nine rules, short maturities and small kappa, OptimalCV, and `ExponentialFittingHestonEngine` with each control variate and two scalings, every row is the parent's to the bit except the 57,703 explicit AsymptoticChF rows at Re psi 0.1 or more, and each of those throws the refusal. Within the bar, 1e-6 of the reference or 5e-7 at an `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` of 1e-8, the refusal takes prices that came in: 19,281 of the grids' 29,484 explicit AsymptoticChF rows on Gauss-Lobatto, Gauss-Kronrod, Gauss-Laguerre and exp-sinh, 1,328 on Gauss-Legendre at 128 nodes, 584 on Simpson, and 5,771 of the 6,912 at 1 day to 7 weeks. AngledContour or OptimalCV prices them within it but for 27 rows, 169 of the Gauss-Legendre rows and 88 rows below 0.15 years, all where AngledContour meets a limit of its own. Every uniform bound above 0.1 leaves a fixed-node rule silently off, Gauss-Laguerre on 346 rows at 1 and on 1,023 at 2, and `ExponentialFittingHestonEngine` at every bound; and taking AngledContour in place of an explicit choice, as OptimalCV does, would override the caller without OptimalCV's other conditions. So the engine refuses an explicit choice it can't price, as at rho = -1 and 1 and for BranchCorrection with an adaptive rule. Three cases' AsymptoticChF rows, which priced within their bars on adaptive rules or were skipped as #342's, now check the refusal, and `testDoCalculationAwayFromTheForward` keeps [#343](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/343)'s check of AsymptoticChF's correction on new rows at Re psi 0.057 and 0.072. Below the bound, where OptimalCV takes AsymptoticChF, `ExponentialFittingHestonEngine`'s default comes out up to 3.5e-5 off on a spot of 100 ([#373](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/373)). |
| [#346](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/346) (2026-11-21) | `ql/math/integrals/kronrodintegral.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/integrals.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `GaussKronrodAdaptive` accepts an interval once its 7-point Gauss and 15-point Kronrod estimates agree to the interval's share of the tolerance, and the two can agree on an interval the integrand isn't resolved on while both are off. On `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::gaussKronrod(1e-8)`, for calls struck at 80 on a spot of 100 against references on exp-sinh at 1e-12, AngledContour three days out came back 1.1e-5 off after 15 evaluations, the whole range accepted on its first estimate; Andersen-Piterbarg two years out came back 5.6e-6 off after 525; and `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine`'s Andersen-Piterbarg form was off on all 13 rows of #346's comment, by 1.4 to 126 times 1e-8 in the integral, the worst by 3.6e-5 in price. `GaussKronrodAdaptive` now takes a minimum depth, 0 by default: an interval fewer than that many halvings below the whole range is split without being evaluated, so at a depth d none wider than 1/2^d of the range is accepted, and an interval too narrow to split is evaluated as at depth 0. `AnalyticHestonEngine::Integration::gaussKronrod` takes it as a new last argument, 3 by default. No engine constructor builds Gauss-Kronrod by default, so only prices a caller asks of `Integration::gaussKronrod` move, `AnalyticHestonEngine`'s and `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine`'s among them; at depth 0 the rule computes what it did, so the conundrum pricer and the LIBOR market model's covariance parametrizations, which build `GaussKronrodAdaptive` themselves, keep their values. The three prices come back within 2.3e-10, and `AnalyticPTDHestonEngine` within 0.011 of the tolerance in the integral on all 13 rows. On two grids of calls, every form at its default `andersenPiterbargEpsilon` against OptimalCV and AngledContour on exp-sinh at 1e-12, in units of the integral's tolerance: on [#341](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/341)'s long grid of 2 to 10 years, at 1e-6, the 14 prices more than ten tolerances off, up to 16,100, come within 0.54; on a short grid of 1 to 91 days the 4 at 1e-8, up to 63, come within 7.0, and the 23 at 1e-6, up to 463, within 7.8; and no price that priced throws, while 3 Gatheral prices on the long grid and 14 and 25 on the short that ran out of the budget of 1,000 now price. Near rho = -1 and 1, on the 124 rows of [#296](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/296)'s table that have a reference, OptimalCV on `gaussKronrod(1e-10, 100000)` brings 14 of the 18 calls it had 2.8e-2 to 1.7e4 times the price off, at 1 - \|rho\| from 1e-3 to 1e-6, within 2.3e-9, throws on two at 1e-6 that were 43.5 and 147 times off, and leaves two 7.6 and 169 times off; from 1e-8 on nothing moves. It costs evaluations: an integral over a range that can be split takes at least 120 at depth 3, against 15, and throws on a `maxEvaluations` below that, while `Integration::gaussKronrod`'s default is 1,000 and no caller in the library or its test suite passes less. At that budget the grids' evaluations rise 10.7 % and 20.7 % on the long grid at 1e-8 and 1e-6, and 1.7 % and 10.7 % on the short; depth 2 leaves 8 of the two-year row's 40 misses and two of the PTD rows, and depth 4 costs 68 % more evaluations on the long grid at 1e-8. On strikes the grids don't hold, 50, 65, 150 and 200, at a budget of 1,000, 8 Gatheral prices at 1e-8 that came within 1.3 to 3.3 tolerances and 3 Andersen-Piterbarg prices at 1e-6 that were 19.6 to 207 off now run out of it, and 83 that threw now price. `GaussKronrodAdaptive` gains a member, its constructor and private recursion a parameter each, and `Integration::gaussKronrod` a parameter, so code compiled against the old headers must be rebuilt. Seven rows of `testAndersenPiterbargLimitAtAverageVariance`, which guards [#339](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/339)'s limit and passed at depth 3 with it taken out, are also priced at a minimum depth of 0. The depth makes an acceptance with both estimates off rarer, not impossible, and #346 stays open: on the short grid at those strikes, 1 to 30 days out, where \|log(F/K)\| is 0.41 to 0.69 and the integrand turns 13 to 21 times within its decay, 9 of the 65 prices more than ten tolerances off at 1e-6 are left, up to 62, at a budget of 1,000, and at 100,000 2 of the 19 at 1e-8, up to 15, and 13 of the 74 at 1e-6, up to 227. |
| [#380](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/380) (2026-11-21) | `ql/pricingengines/vanilla/analytichestonengine.cpp`, `test-suite/hestonmodel.cpp` | `AnalyticHestonEngine::priceVanillaPayoff` integrates exp-sinh over u = s x, with s = 0.25 / sqrt(vAvg t / 2) held between 0.25 and 1,000, and exp-sinh is the only rule that takes s. It chose s by the form the engine was built with, and took s = 1 for OptimalCV, though OptimalCV prices with the form it resolves to, which is AngledContour at 0.15 years and less and wherever AsymptoticChF's other conditions fail. There OptimalCV on exp-sinh integrated AngledContour's integrand, with AngledContour's control variate and limit, unscaled. On [#377](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/377)'s grid of calls (spot 100, r 0.5 %, q 3 %, 1e-6 to 0.1 years; 2,761 rows with references cross-checked to 1e-10) it was more than 1e-6 off on 7 rows and more than 1e-8 off on 16, the same to the bit at every tolerance from 1e-8 to 1e-13, where AngledContour on exp-sinh at 1e-13 was within 1.3e-15 of the references on all of them; the seven were at the money at v0 1e-4, where s is at its cap, 0.30 % to 0.41 % of the price off. s now goes by the resolved form, so where OptimalCV takes AngledContour it is scaled as AngledContour is, and prices as AngledContour does, to the bit, and where it takes AsymptoticChF, which no form scales, nothing moves. Evaluations move both ways: at 1e-8 the new case's two rows take 484 where they took 1,284, and 360 where they took 268. It took t1 - d as written and divided by sigma^2, so where sigma phi is small against kappa, t1 - d cancelled and 1 / sigma^2 carried the rounding into the exponent: at sigma 1e-7 and kappa 1, ten-year calls came back up to 0.089 off with the Gatheral formula and 0.12 with AndersenPiterbarg, on Gauss-Laguerre's 144 nodes, the default, and where sigma^2 underflows to 0 the price was NaN. Each interval now takes its solution about the root D_- = nu / (t1 + d), which doesn't cancel, with 1 - e^{-d tau} from expm1 and the logarithm from log1p; only D_-'s other form, (t1 - d) / sigma^2, divides by sigma^2, and the engine takes it only where t1 - d doesn't cancel. At ordinary sigma prices move by rounding: on #344's 1,500 three-interval models at 91 days to two years by at most 3.2e-8, and of the 268 calls that move by more than 1e-12, the 262 with a reference better than 3.5e-8 are closer to it on 241 to 258, by configuration, and where further, by at most 6.0e-10. There the engine takes 6.8 % fewer instructions with the Gatheral formula on Gauss-Laguerre and 3.2 % fewer with AndersenPiterbarg, and 34 % fewer on Gauss-Lobatto and 60 % fewer on exp-sinh, where the adaptive rules stop sooner. #360's option 1 as the issue wrote it still divides by sigma^2, so it is NaN where sigma^2 is 0, and at small kappa it builds D by cancelling D_- against the decaying part: at kappa 1e-7 the new case's call came back 3.5e-9 off, relative. One corner stays NaN, as the code's comment says: where sigma^2 is 0, which takes sigma below about 2.2e-162, and Re t1 isn't positive, which takes a kappa below \|rho\| sigma, D_-'s other form divides by 0, so only a kappa below 2.2e-162 reaches it. The same division by sigma^2 is left in `HestonRNDCalculator`'s characteristic function, and so in `AnalyticPDFHestonEngine` ([#386](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/386)), and in the closed forms of the two geometric Asian Heston engines ([#384](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/384)). | [#385](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/385) (2026-11-22) | `ql/pricingengines/asian/analytic_discr_geom_av_price_heston.cpp`, `test-suite/asianoptions.cpp` | `AnalyticDiscreteGeometricAveragePriceAsianHestonEngine`'s characteristic function sums log F(z1, z2, tau) over the steps between fixings, with F = cosh(x) + (kappa - z2 sigma^2) sinh(x) / temp, x = tau temp / 2 and temp = sqrt(kappa^2 - 2 z1 sigma^2). F returned 1 + (kappa - z2 sigma^2) / 2 wherever \|kappa^2 - 2 sigma^2\| < 1e-8. That tested the wrong quantity, since kappa^2 - 2 sigma^2 is temp^2 only at z1 = 1 and z1 is complex, and it dropped tau. So on the line kappa^2 = 2 sigma^2, and in the corner kappa < 1e-4, sigma < 7.07e-5, every price went through a wrong F. On #385's market kappa 1 and sigma sqrt(1/2) priced -8.766 where its neighbours price 4.6219, the corner's rows priced from -48.52 to 10.464 against Black-Scholes's 5.1272, and `MCDiscreteArithmeticAPHestonEngine`, whose control variate is this engine's price, priced 0 on the line. Where temp is exactly 0, at kappa = 0 on the step from the last fixing to expiry, the engine priced NaN. F now takes sinh(x) / temp from the series of sinh(x) / x, (tau / 2)(1 + x^2 / 6), wherever \|x\| < 1e-4, and keeps its expression elsewhere. 6,480 options on one, 12 and 26 fixings price bit for bit as before; the line's prices are smooth through it; the corner is within 3.1e-6 of Black-Scholes at sigma 1e-6 up to kappa 1e-4; and kappa = 0 prices within 1.3e-13 of kappa 1e-12. | [#98](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/98) (2026-11-24) | `ql/processes/blackscholesprocess.{hpp,cpp}`, `test-suite/blackscholesprocess.cpp` | `GeneralizedBlackScholesProcess::localVolatility()` rebuilt the local volatility it derives from the Black volatility at its first call after every notification of the process, a spot, rate or dividend tick or a move of the evaluation date included, and relinked its handle, which notified the handle's observers. The process's `drift()`, `diffusion()`, `evolve()` and `expectation()` read it, and so does `blackScholesTheta`, which `AnalyticDiscreteGeometricAveragePriceAsianEngine` and `BinomialVanillaEngine` call on every price. A spot tick followed by `localVolatility()->localVol()` then makes no allocation where it made 9, and on a `BlackConstantVol` runs 754 instructions where it ran 3,121; in `linux-ci-build-with-nonstandard-options`' configuration, the thread-safe observer among its flags, it makes 6 allocations where it made 72. The geometric Asian engine's NPV after a spot tick makes 6 allocations where it made 15, and the binomial, finite-difference and Monte Carlo engines the issue names make 9 fewer, a small share of theirs. | [#90](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/90) (2026-11-24) | `ql/termstructures/{bootstraphelper,iterativebootstrap}.hpp`, ten helper sources, `test-suite/piecewiseyieldcurve.cpp` | `IterativeBootstrap::calculate()` hands its curve to every alive helper's `setTermStructure()` on every rebuild. Each of the 16 overrides that links a handle to the curve built a `shared_ptr` to it with a `null_deleter`, which allocates a control block, and passed it to `RelinkableHandle::linkTo()`, which found the pointer and the observer flag unchanged and did nothing. `calculate()` also allocated its bounds and attempt counts, three vectors, at the start of every pass, and a curve with a global interpolation copied its nodes into a fourth once a rebuild. `detail::linkToBootstrapped()`, a function template in `bootstraphelper.hpp` that the 16 overrides call, now links a helper's handle only when the handle is empty or links another object, and only then builds the pointer, so a handle is relinked, and its observers notified, exactly when it was before. The bounds, the attempt counts and the copy of the nodes are mutable members of `IterativeBootstrap`, reset at every pass as the locals were, which keep their storage from one pass and one rebuild to the next. A quote tick and a discount factor on a curve of 3 deposits and 6 swaps make 30 allocations where they made 42, and on a curve of 9 OIS helpers 432 where they made 444; the rest are the coupons' copies of their index's name ([#89](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/89)). Every node, exception and notification count in the scripted histories of the commit's probe, the global loop's and the retries' included, prints the same bytes before and after. One case behaves differently: a handle that a derived helper linked to the same curve as an observer was relinked without observing at the next rebuild, and now stays as it was; no helper in the library does that. `IterativeBootstrap` grows by four `std::vector`s, 96 bytes, and so do the five piecewise curves that hold it by value, so code compiled against the old headers must be rebuilt. The issue's third item, keeping the deposit helper's dates, isn't taken: it would make the helper a friend of `IborIndex`, skip a derived index's override of `forecastFixing`, and save about 1.6 % of a rebuild's instructions. |

On a 30-year semiannual bond, a `yearFraction` over a coupon period makes no allocation and runs 306 instructions where it made 1 and ran 2,246, `BondFunctions::cleanPrice` from a yield none and 62,621 where it made 78 and ran 213,533, and `BondFunctions::yield` from a clean price none where it made 917.3 on average. A change to any calendar sends a day counter built before it to the slow path for good, where a call makes the parent's allocations and runs 16 to 322 instructions more than the parent's ([#410](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/410)); the class documentation advises building the day counter once the holidays are set. `ActualActual::ISMA_Impl`, private and built only in `actualactual.cpp`, grows; `ActualActual`'s layout doesn't change. |
| [#97](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/97) (2026-11-24) | `ql/cashflows/couponpricer.{hpp,cpp}`, `ql/cashflows/iborcoupon.cpp`, `ql/instruments/swap.cpp`, `test-suite/cashflows.cpp` | Pricing a swap of Ibor coupons again did four things for each coupon that it didn't need. `BlackIborCouponPricer::initialize`, which a floating coupon calls each time it works out its rate, worked out the forecast curve's discount factor at the payment date, though only the pricer's four prices read it and nothing in the library calls them; `IborCoupon::indexFixing()` and the coupon's accessors for its fixing's dates cast the coupon's pricer before the pricer's own check found the dates worked out; `Swap::setupArguments` assigned the swap's legs to the engine's arguments, a reference count up and down for each cash flow, even where they already held the same cash flows; and `IborCouponPricer::initialize` assigned the coupon's index even where the pricer already held it. The pricer now works the discount factor out in a protected `discount()`, which the four prices call first, and the member `discount_` goes; the coupon checks whether its dates are worked out before the cast; the legs are copied only where they differ; and the index is assigned only where it differs. A quote tick and a discount factor on a curve of 3 deposits and 6 swaps on Euribor 6M run 1,949,889.5 instructions where they ran 2,285,682.5, and a 5-year Euribor 6M swap's NPV started 2 months ago 46,225.5 where it ran 52,965.5. A caller sees three changes. With the default timing adjustment, an Ibor coupon fixed in advance and paid after its forecast curve's last date has a rate and an amount, where both threw the curve's range error, and with its fixing stored it has its rate where the curve's bootstrap fails. A forecast curve that only coupons with stored fixings read stays uncalculated, so in the default lazy-object mode its notifications don't reach those coupons until something reads it; it is also bootstrapped less often, so its values can move within the bootstrap's accuracy. And once a pricer has worked out a coupon's dates, the coupon gives them where its own pricer is unset or isn't an Ibor pricer, where it threw. Every other value is the parent's to the bit. `BlackIborCouponPricer` shrinks from 272 to 256 bytes and `BlackIborQuantoCouponPricer` from 304 to 288, so code compiled against the old header must be rebuilt. The pricer's cast of the coupon stays, as the check that the coupon is an `IborCoupon`, and so do `VanillaSwap`'s casts of its coupons, which don't run under `DiscountingSwapEngine`. |
| [#412](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/412) (2026-11-24) | `ql/cashflow.hpp`, `ql/cashflows/{coupon.hpp,cashflows.cpp}`, `test-suite/cashflows.cpp` | `CashFlows` told a coupon from another cash flow with `ext::dynamic_pointer_cast<Coupon>` at 16 places in `cashflows.cpp`, among them `startDate`, `maturityDate` and `npvbps`, which `DiscountingSwapEngine` calls on every leg at every calculation: each cast went through `__dynamic_cast` and built a `shared_ptr`, to find out again what doesn't change once a leg is built. `CashFlow` now has a public virtual `asCoupon()`, which returns null; `Coupon` overrides it, `final`, to return itself, so it gives what the cast gave for every class derived from `CashFlow`. The 16 places call it and keep a raw pointer, which they use only while the leg holds the cash flow. On top of #97's changes, a quote tick and a discount factor on the curve of 3 deposits and 6 swaps run 1,162,332.5 instructions where they ran 1,949,889.5, and on a curve of 9 OIS helpers on ESTR 1,522,662.5 where they ran 2,418,918.5; the Euribor swap's NPV runs 29,604.5 where it ran 46,225.5, and a 5-year ESTR OIS's at spot 26,450.5 where it ran 36,831.5. Every value is the same to the bit. The new virtual changes every cash flow's vtable, so code compiled against the old headers must be rebuilt; no object's layout changes. The other 24 casts to `Coupon` in `ql/`, in 11 files, stay, as do the overnight pricer's casts of the coupon it prices and of that coupon's index ([#414](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/414)). |

On top of #412's changes, a quote tick and a discount factor on the curve of 9 OIS helpers on ESTR run 1,342,248.5 instructions where they ran 1,522,662.5, and a 5-year ESTR OIS's NPV at spot 23,324.5 where it ran 26,450.5; a compounded ESTR leg capped and floored on the Black pricer runs 63,038.5 where it ran 67,981.5; an averaged OIS runs 2,530,572.5 where it ran 2,535,222.5, its daily forecasts taking almost all of it ([#416](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/416)). The two probes' 15,480 values are the parent's to the bit. The new virtual changes every floating coupon's vtable, and the new member makes `OvernightIndexedCoupon` 464 bytes, from 448, so code compiled against the old headers must be rebuilt; the soname doesn't change. The engine's casts stay ([#96](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/96)), as does a capped coupon's cast of its pricer ([#417](https://github.com/dudleylane/QuantLib/issues/417)). |

Where it and its underlying coupon share a Black pricer, as the coupons of a leg that `OvernightLeg` builds with `withCouponPricer`, or that `setCouponPricer` gives a pricer, do, the underlying's rate was worked out twice, in the underlying's calculation and again in the capped coupon's `initialize`, since a Black pricer's `initialize` works the swaplet out; the coupon cast its pricer with `ext::dynamic_pointer_cast`; `optionletRateGlobal` copied the coupon's fixing dates into a new vector; and `optionletRateLocal` asked the index for each past day's fixing, and today's, with `Index::pastFixing`, once for the cap and once for the floor, each call checking the date against the fixing calendar and looking the history up by the index's name. The capped coupon now leaves its own `initialize` out where the pricer holds what initializing it on this coupon would give, and tells the two Black pricers by their exact type, casting any other pricer as before; `optionletRateGlobal` binds the fixing dates by const reference; and `optionletRateLocal` reads the index's stored history once for each cap or floor, as the swaplet does. On top of #414's changes, the NPV of a 5-year ESTR swap whose leg is capped and floored on the Black pricers runs 43,528.5 instructions a tick where it ran 63,038.5 with the cap and floor on a compounded coupon's rate and 65,171.5 where 208,012.5 on each day's rate, and on an averaged leg 2,545,452.5 where 5,066,200.5 and 2,564,455.5 where 5,208,898.5; it allocates 1, 3, 2 and 4 times a tick where it allocated 12, 136, 14 and 138. The probes' values and errors are the parent's to the bit, but for 192 of the third probe's 394,528: the rates of coupons capped or floored on each day's rate, on an index whose `pastFixing` gives other rates than it stores, now bound the stored fixings, as their swaplets do.
